Chapter 319: A True Immortal’s Written Legacy

Frankly speaking, the Wei family wasn’t particularly delighted; instead, they initially suspected that Ji Yuan might be deceiving them.

If he truly were deceiving them, Wei Xuan and Wei Ming couldn’t be certain whether Yan Fei was involved, so even if they had some doubts, they tried their best to speak gently.

“Uh, Mr. Ji, are you certain this book is a biography titled ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds’? Our Wei family has passed it down for generations, and the elders all said it was an extraordinary martial arts manual, a famous ancestral treasure of our Wei family…”

Ji Yuan smiled faintly, looking at Wei Xuan as he spoke.

“It seems your family’s records are mistaken. This book is neither a martial arts manual, nor was its author surnamed Wei. Instead, his name was Zhong Pingxiu, and seems to have been a cultivator from overseas.”

“Huh? Not surnamed Wei? “

Not only were the members of the Wei family nearby stunned, but even Yan Fei and Niu Batian were momentarily taken aback.

Wei Xuan frowned, looking at Ji Yuan. He was afraid that Ji Yuan was about to say that he happened to know Zhong Pingxiu’s descendants.1

“Everyone, there’s no need to be anxious. Although I am interested in your Wordless Heavenly Book, I would not resort to fabricating lies to deceive you. If the Wei family allows it, I shall simply sit here in the hall and peruse it for a while. “

Ultimately, they were still guests from afar. Although the Wei family harbored misgivings in their hearts, they showed none of it on their faces. Wei Xuan laughed heartily.

“It’s no trouble at all, Sir,” Wei Xuan said. “For many years, no one has been able to discern the characters in this book. Today, we are overjoyed to learn that you are able to do so, Sir! Please, sir, take your time to peruse it here in the hall. However, our Wei family also has an impertinent request…”

Yan Fei frowned and looked at the Wei family, especially Wei Xuan and Wei Ming; it was clear that these two did not entirely trust Mr. Ji. Niu Batian merely scoffed and said nothing, though he too was quite interested in ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds’.

Before Wei Xuan could finish his sentence, Ji Yuan had already guessed his intent. However, if Ji Yuan were to directly finish his words, the Wei family might grow suspicious again, so he waited patiently.

“We merely hope that, after you have read it, Sir, you might recount the contents of the Heavenly Wordless Book to us, so that we might also know what what was written in our family’s ancestral book. “

“Well, alright. However, recounting it might lead to an incomplete recollection. How about I write it down for you instead? “

Ji Yuan’s suggestion was naturally met with unanimous agreement from the Wei family.

“Quickly, go fetch the gentleman a brush, ink, paper, and inkstone…”

After Wei Xuan gave instructions to his servants, he turned back and respectfully addressed Ji Yuan.

“Sir, please sit down and read at your leisure. If you were to copy down our Wei family’s treasured ancestral book, our Wei family will be eternally grateful!”

Wei Xuan maintained his impeccable courtesy, but only he knew how much genuine respect actually lay behind his words. Furthermore, even though Ji Yuan had clearly stated that Zhong Pingxiu was the author, Wei Xuan still referred to it as something left by the Wei family’s ancestors, simply avoiding saying it was written by their ancestors.

Ji Yuan didn’t call him out. He nodded and sat down in his seat, book in hand, and began to read intently. Meanwhile, Yan Fei and the others were left in silence.

A moment later, a servant arrived carrying a tray. Upon it were the prepared Four Treasures of the Study, alongside a thick stack of Xuan paper.2

Without even lifting his head, Ji Yuan reached out and tapped twice on the tea table, indicating for the Four Treasures of the Study to be placed there.

Yan Fei proactively took the tray from the Wei family’s servant, helped arrange the Four Treasures of the Study, stacked the Xuan paper neatly, and personally ground ink for Ji Yuan. This attitude was indeed that of a junior respectfully aiding an important elder.

Still without looking up, Ji Yuan placed the book in Heavenly Script on the table and, as he flipped through the pages, reached for a brush. In a natural movement, he dipped the brush into the inkstone, and began writing directly on the rice paper, which was held down by a paperweight.

His characters were small, yet exceedingly delicate, almost as if the small script were being magnified in a blend of divine technique and pure skill. Even after merely writing the first few characters, the Wei family could tell that Mr. Ji definitely a true master of calligraphy.

As the saying went, “To observe one’s writing is to observe one’s character.” Ji Yuan’s ability to write with such divine and masterful handwriting subtly elevated his status in the hearts of the Wei family members.

“Riding the gentle breeze and leisurely clouds, I wander across thousands of mountains and countless rivers, behold hundreds of rivers and streams, circle through towering peaks… Carrying the dazzling starlight, I observe the scenery of each season, all as one…”

As Ji Yuan wrote, everyone’s attention gradually began to focus on the content he was writing down. These were merely simple characters, devoid of the true essence contained within the Heavenly Script. Even so, feeling of utter freedom and leisurely ease spontaneously arose in everyone’s hearts.

“Observing the wondrous Celestial River above, delving into the profound mysteries of the abyss below… As immortal cultivators, this is the realm our hearts yearn for, the realm our spirits aspire to…”

Ji Yuan continued to write, gradually letting his audience realize that the protagonist within this text seemed to be an immortal.

Yan Fei and Niu Batian were naturally unsurprised; after all, this Wordless Heavenly Book was no ordinary tome. The members of the Wei family, however, glanced at each other, but soon enough, their attention was drawn back to the paper.

Ji Yuan paid no mind to anyone else’s thoughts. He had originally assumed that ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds’ was merely a simple personal travelogue, despite being penned by a cultivator, and that it was ultimately different from an ordinary biography, aside from being perhaps more authentic.

But now, as he read, Ji Yuan’s spiritual consciousness seemed to wander alongside the words in Heavenly Script, roaming between heaven and earth. That feeling of freedom and unbound ease seemed to flow from the book itself, a mysterious power allowing his mind and spirit to wander freely.

Almost involuntarily, his consciousness within his inner spiritual world manifested the majestic beauty of ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds,’ riding the gentle breeze and treading on white clouds, wandering in a trance between heaven and earth.

It was as if his mind and body had split into two distinct entities at this moment: one, flipping through the book and writing with divine inspiration, the other lost in a trance, dreamlike, wandering through profound mysteries within his inner world.

“Yan… Brother Yan… look around you…”

Niu Batian suddenly nudged Yan Fei’s arm, his voice a low whisper, causing the latter to shift his gaze from the paper. Upon looking around, he instantly stiffened.

The Wei family members also heard Niu Batian’s words and looked up, glancing around. The next moment, everyone’s mouths fell open, speechless.

They were clearly still in the main hall of the mansion, but a faint layer of mist seemed to be rising.

Through the mist, they could see that their surroundings had transformed into another vast world. Here, there were no walls, no carved railings, and no tiles overhead to obscure their view. The spiritual beauty of heaven and earth was laid bare before them.

“Rumble…”

The faint sound of heavenly thunder could be heard from up above, and even Yan Fei and Niu Batian couldn’t help but raise their hands and duck, not to mention the Wei family members’ reactions.

“Splash-crash-shhhhh…”

The blue seas churned with the tides, and as waves rose, water crashed everywhere.

Everyone felt as though they were riding in a small boat. The surrounding scenery shifted rapidly, and time flowed unpredictably around them. There was heavy snow, green leaves and red flowers, glittering stars, flashes of lightning and rumbling thunder…

“Alas, I rose and looked around, but my joy was in vain; it was all just a dream!”

As Ji Yuan made his final brushstroke, the strange phenomena surrounding them vanished in an instant. He, alongside Yan Fei and the others, “woke up.”

“Excellent book, a wonderful book! While this book is not a profound immortal technique, it is nonetheless a treasure! Of all the books in Heavenly Script that I have ever seen, this one is the best!”

Ji Yuan’s was currently filled with both excitement and deep emotion. He couldn’t help but stand up and pace a few steps back and forth, his gaze occasionally drifting to this copy of ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds’.

Ji Yuan’s praise was spontaneous, and naturally, everyone heard it clearly. Yan Fei and Niu Batian were astonished by Mr. Ji’s incredibly high praise, particularly the latter.

The Old Bull was very clear about Mr. Ji’s true identity: a supreme immortal cultivator who could share wine and chat happily with True Dragons, guiding all beings under heaven.

Just how remarkable must this book in Heavenly Script be, to receive such praise from Ji Yuan?

Ji Yuan was feeling exceptionally happy at the moment. The sense of carefree freedom and relaxation captured within ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds’ was almost like another version of ‘Xiaoyao You.’ However, when Ji Yuan spoke of ‘Xiaoyao You,’ it was to express its principles through his own understanding. This book of Heavenly Script, however, allowed Ji Yuan to truly feel the spirit and intent of its author, experiencing a profound impact on his state of mind and understanding.

“The author of this book must be, at the very least, a True Immortal! “

Ji Yuan was now absolutely certain: this Zhong Pingxiu was definitely a True Immortal master, or perhaps even higher; otherwise, he could never have imbued such divine essence into the book.

A faint rumble of thunder sounded, originating not from outside the mansion, but from within Ji Yuan himself.

“Rumble… Rumble…”

Amidst everyone’s astonishment, Ji Yuan gently waved his hand, temporarily suppressing the thunder. With the roar of thunder, Ji Yuan could no longer afford to hesitate or focus on anything else, so he turned to look at the Wei family.

“Would you be willing to part with this ‘Dream of Wandering in the Clouds’? Or perhaps lend it to me for a while? I will generously reward you when I return it!”

“This…”

The Wei family members hesitated slightly. This book, left by their ancestors, was clearly extremely mystical. Just now, when Mr. Ji was reading, strange phenomena actually rose from within it; it must surely be an immortal scripture. How could the Wei family feel at ease lending such a book to someone else?

Although this Mr. Ji, who could see the characters in the book, was certainly no ordinary person, wasn’t it also true that this extraordinary person cared unusually deeply about this book?

“Sir, this is a treasured heirloom passed down through our Wei family. Although we have always generously allowed fellow martial artists to view it, we have never lent it out, and outsiders would never make such a request. We are naturally grateful for your assistance in transcribing the words from this book, but please, do not to put us in a difficult position…”

Although Ji Yuan had been immersed in the book just now, that didn’t mean he was unaware of what was happening outside. Even after all this, the Wei family still didn’t trust him. However, ultimately, the book still belonged to them. Being so flatly rejected, his recent joy was somewhat dampened.

Ji Yuan’s expression was stern, but he said nothing. He simply glanced at the Wei family members for a moment.

“Indeed, it is truly a treasured heirloom of the Wei family. Then I shall not trouble you further.

Before he had even finished his words, clouds and mist rose from beneath Ji Yuan’s feet. A gentle breeze swept through the room, and in an instant, he rode the clouds and flew off into the sky.

He did indeed wish to read ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds’ a few more times, but the most crucial part was this first spiritual journey. A little extra polish would be nice,3 but it wasn’t necessary.

Only now did Niu Batian and Yan Fei jolt awake, realize what was happening, and hurry out the door, shouting towards the sky.

“Master Ji! Where are you going? “

“I have gained some insights, and must choose a place to explore these profound mysteries. Our journey together ends here. May we meet again if fate allows!”

Ji Yuan’s voice drifted back, his form already having vanished into the sky. Niu Batian had a mind to pursue him, but upon hearing Ji Yuan’s words, he stopped in his tracks. He could only glare resentfully at the Wei family.

“Ah!”

Chapter 320: Wandering Without End

Speaking of resentment, the Old Bull felt that he was the most miserable party here. As he stared at the masters and servants of the Wei family, his eyes blazed with an anger, bloodshot and quite terrifying.

Had it not been for Mr. Ji’s recent departure via cloud, the Niu Batian of before wouldn’t have cared one big whether the Wei family had a ‘valid excuse’ for their actions — after all, the Old Bull himself would also have a ‘valid excuse’ to grow furious and go on a killing spree!

But after only a few breaths, he forcefully suppressed his anger. After all, his journey with Ji Yuan hadn’t been in vain. The key was that he was very afraid of Ji Yuan. Still, he couldn’t help but curse under his breath.

“Damn it all to hell! This Old Bull’s immortal guidance… Ah! It’s gone…”

Yan Fei also felt a little disappointed, but he was better off than Niu Batian, precisely because he believed Mr. Ji had already imparted immensely valuable principles onto him – the “Sword Intent Inscription,” his confidence in returning to the martial path, and his belief in breaking through.

The Wei family members had not yet fully recovered from their shock. They had initially been staring blankly at the sky, but now, spurred on by Niu Batian’s aura, many were now nervously watching the bloodshot-eyed Niu Batian.

In that instant, this simple, honest-looking man had erupted with astonishing ferocity, overwhelming all the members of the Wei family. They were all on guard, convinced that this individual was also a terrifying master.

But ultimately, it was Ji Yuan’s departure by cloud that had the greatest impact on the Wei family.

Wei Xuan’s heart raced, and his expression was somewhat agitated. He shifted his gaze away from Niu Batian, looked at Yan Fei, and carefully inquired.

“Hero Yan, that elder of yours, he just… flew away on a cloud. Is he perhaps an immortal being? ”

Yan Fei crossed his arms, clasping his longsword to his chest, and smiled at Wei Xuan and Wei Ming.

“You have eyes, do you still need to ask me that? My visit today was primarily to bring Mr. Ji to view your Wei family’s ‘Wordless Heavenly Book.’ Since this matter is concluded, I shall take my leave as well. ”

After Yan Fei finished speaking, he patted Niu Batian’s shoulder.

“Brother Niu, shall we go? ”

Yan Fei didn’t want to stay here for another moment, and the Old Bull was even less inclined to do so, glad to avoid stewing in his anger here.

“Haha, alright, Mr. Ji has left, there’s no point staying here anymore. ”

Wei Xuan quickly gave his son a meaningful look. Wei Ming nodded and stepped forward quickly.

“Hero Yan, Master Niu, please stay. Our Wei family was unaware just now and inadvertently offended the immortal. Will that immortal be returning? Please, both of you must stay and allow our Wei family to fulfill our duties as hosts. That gentleman is Hero Yan’s elder, surely…”

“No need! ”

Yan Fei raised a hand to stop Wei Ming’s polite words, finding them somewhat irritating.

He turned back to look inside the main hall, where a disciple of the Wei family was currently placing the ‘Wordless Heavenly Book’ back into its wooden box and covering it.

“Although I have always considered Mr. Ji my teacher, there is a distinction between immortals and mortals. It has been over a decade since we last met, and I do not know if we are fated to meet each other again in this lifetime… I won’t trouble you further. Besides, Brother Niu has a rather bad temper, and his martial arts are more than ten times better than mine. Would the Wei family truly be at ease if we stayed here?”

Yan Fei’s words caused Wei Xuan and Wei Ming to freeze for a moment. Before Wei Ming could say anything else, Yan Fei smiled at Niu Batian, and the two walked out of the estate together.

“Hero Yan! Hero Yan, what are you talking about! ”

Wei Ming hurriedly gave chase. His father, Wei Xuan, lifted his foot but ultimately did not step forward.

Once the two outsiders were gone, some of the members of the Wei family nearby angrily began tussling.

“Oh no! If only we had let him borrow the book just now, would we have received personal guidance from the immortal? ”

“Of course!”

“I can’t believe our Wei family’s Heavenly Wordless Book to truly be a heavenly book…”

Wei Xuan snorted coldly, and the rustling sounds around him immediately ceased. Of course he regretted it, but as the head of the Wei family, how could he stand being the scapegoat?

“Now that it has come to this, what’s the point of arguing any further? if the immortal returns to borrow it again, I will naturally offer up the Heavenly Book to him with both hands. For now, we at least have the immortal’s handwritten translation!”

Wei Xuan’s words successfully shifted everyone’s attention, quickly bringing them out of their despondency and regret. They began to feel a surge of excitement. Many had already rushed back into the hall.

On the table in the hall lay over a dozen sheets of Xuan paper stacked in a pile, each page filled with small calligraphy.

In the eyes of the Wei family, this undoubtedly exceeded the realm of ordinary martial arts manuals; this was a miraculous divine technique left behind by an immortal. If they could obtain this technique, then even immortal cultivation might be within reach.

At this moment, Wei Ming was still chasing after Yan Fei and Niu Batian, hoping to persuade them to stay, saying that he felt that the matter shouldn’t end on such a note today. However, Yan Fei and Niu Batian paid him no mind and simply kept walking forward, heads held high.

However, after walking down a stretch of road outside the Wei family mansion, Yan Fei paused, gazing back towards the main hall, and spoke to Wei Ming, who had been following them all this time.

“Wei Ming, I’ll give you a few final words. If today’s events were to spread outside, it might not be good for the Wei family. The ‘Wordless Heavenly Book’ used to be just a rumor in the martial arts world, but now that an immortal has read it and even left a translation, it is no longer a matter of mere speculation.”

“Hero Yan, you…”

Wei Ming looked a little nervous.

“Rest assured, although I do not claim to dedicate myself to righteousness and chivalry, I disdain such acts. I have no interest in that heavenly book in your home, nor will I spread word of it. ”

The Old Bull beside him was already growing impatient. He directly pulled Yan Fei along and headed out.

“Let’s go, let’s go, Brother Yan, why bother with them? Even if you never see Mr. Ji again, this Old Bull will live a long time. If you pass away someday, I’ll definitely invite Mr. Ji to visit your grave and offer some incense or something. ”

Hearing this, Yan Fei’s mouth twitched, feeling both exasperated and amused.

“Hahaha, Brother Yan, come on, let’s go to the Soft Jade Building in Luoqing City! Yesterday, this Old Bull here met many close confidantes; I’ll introduce you! ”

Old Niu laughed lewdly, dragging Yan Fei towards the city, and soon disappeared from Wei Ming’s sight.

Wei Ming watched for a while, then sighed and turned back alongside his servants. However, rather than feeling disappointed, the Wei family on the whole felt that today was a day of good fortune. After taking a few steps back, smiles began to bloom on their faces.

In the main hall of the mansion, all the Wei family members, led by Wei Xuan, gathered around the table. Wei Xuan had picked up the ‘Wordless Heavenly Book’ and was flipping through it, comparing it to the words Ji Yuan had left on the table. It looked as though he could see the handwriting of the book written in Heavenly Script.

When Wei Ming returned, those gathered naturally made room for him.

“Ming’er, you’re back? Where are Yan Fei and that fellow surnamed Niu? ”

Wei Xuan glanced at Wei Ming, then once again turned his attention to the table.

“Hero Yan and Hero Niu have left. However, before Great Hero Yan departed, he warned us that if word of the immortal that helped us translate the heavenly book were to spread, it could bring about a fatal calamity for our family.”

“Of course.”

Wei Xuan nodded in deep agreement, then looked up at the surrounding Wei family members and servants.

“Not a single word of today’s events can be mentioned to anyone. Otherwise, if this draws unwanted attention, our relatives and families will be in danger. However, with this immortal’s handwritten copy of ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds,’ there’s no need to worry about the Wei family’s prosperity!”

Wei Xuan declared with absolute certainty. The surrounding members of the Wei family, including Wei Ming, shared a look of pride and excitement, as if basking in the glory.

Wei Xuan examined the heavenly book for a while, but ultimately couldn’t discern any characters. He returned the book to its box and began to carefully examine the handwritten manuscript on the table alongside his family members, its ink still fresh.

“Just this calligraphy alone… is already priceless…”

One of the Wei family’s elders murmured, a sentiment which deeply resonated with everyone in the room. Even those members of the Wei family who were uneducated or disliked writing found that the sight of this calligraphy made them feel extremely comfortable.

…

On the road between the Wei family estate and Luoqing City, Niu Batian and Yan Fei were walking back towards the city.

“Brother Niu, do you think that copy of ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds’ that Mr. Ji copied for the Wei family has the power to bring good fortune to the Wei family?”

Regarding whether the Wei family could keep today a secret, Yan Fei actually thought they might be able to. After all, the Wei family lived alone outside the city, isolated and internally stable, so they must have significant control over their people.

“Brother Yan, Mr. Ji’s didn’t have any real intentions in leaving this writing. The Wei family is, after all, ultimately mortal, so it’s unlikely that they will be able to ascend to heaven in one step just by relying on this script. However, this calligraphy was written by Mr. Ji himself, and furthermore, he had been so immersed in reading the book just now that he even manifested strange visions of the changes in heaven and earth. That manuscript must be somewhat extraordinary. Before I left, this Old Bull used a technique to observe that document carefully, and I could indeed see a fleeting glimmer of light. However, this Old Bull’s cultivation is still too shallow to see through it. ”

The Old Bull was not mistaken: Ji Yuan indeed only intended to leave the manuscript without injecting any true intent, but during the process of reading, his spirit and will involuntarily merged, and some of his profound essence naturally flowed into the handwritten text. Even Ji Yuan himself was unaware of this. After reading the book, he felt enlightened, and when he failed to borrow the book, he simply used his flight technique to leave.

“In that case, although the Wei family annoyed Mr. Ji this time and cut off their chance to become immortals, have they actually still been blessed?”

Hearing Yan Fei’s question, the Old Bull’s simple, honest face broke into a smile.

“You could say that. If the Wei family is careful enough, a long-term contemplation of the text will certainly bring some benefits.”

The Old Bull actually left one final thing unsaid: in the current era, the Wei family needed to guard against more than just mortals.

The Wei family members might be able to keep silent to outsiders, but what about their own family members, when they were studying the immortal’s handwritten text within their home? Perhaps they might even hold some kind of ritual sacrifice or incense burning ceremony.

What if other immortal cultivators, ghosts, demons, or monsters learned of this matter? The first might be alright, but in the latter cases, it would be like a child holding gold in their hands, exposing it to everyone.1

…

As the Old Bull and Yan Fei were returning to Luoqing City, Ji Yuan was flying rapidly through the sky on his cloud.

Ji Yuan had no intention of finding a place nearby to meditate. Firstly, the land of Zuyue was quite chaotic, with ghosts and demons frequently emerging, making it unsuitable for quiet cultivation; secondly, what he had just comprehended at this moment did not align with this kind of practice.

However, Ji Yuan had no intentions of returning to Dazhen.

Instead, he continued eastward. The most suitable place for secluded cultivation at this moment was the boundless sea; that was where the true meaning of ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds’ truly resided. A portion of Zuyue’s territory bordered the Eastern Sea.

Half a day later, Ji Yuan could already smell the sea breeze. His pale, ancient eyes gazed far into the distance, and faintly, he could already make out the blurry blue of the ocean.

His mental world was filled with images from ‘A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds,’ and Ji Yuan muttered excerpts of ‘Xiaoyao You’ to himself, which he considered his own perception of the wonders of an immortal’s travels.

“…those who follow the natural righteousness of Heaven and Earth, control the changes of the six Qi, and wander without end…”2

T/n: I like how this instance really just solidifies one of JY’s defining characteristics: his wanderlust and sheer wonder at the world around him. His style of cultivation is truly to simply wander.

The English equivalent would be “like taking candy from a baby”

Excerpt from the Xiaoyao You. As per usual with these kinds of texts, this is just one of the many possible interpretations and translations

Chapter 321: Calm Winds, Scattered Clouds

Strictly speaking, this was Ji Yuan’s first glimpse of the ocean in this life.

The coastline drew nearer, Ji Yuan simply glanced over the coastal landscape and villages, before flying ceaselessly into the boundless ocean.

Even though he was no longer the ordinary man he had been in his previous life, he still felt a sense of his own insignificance rise within him as he stood between the sea and the sky, amidst the vastness of the universe.

At this moment, Ji Yuan deliberately lowered his altitude, placing himself beneath the clouds. Below him lay the surging azure sea; above him, clouds unfurled and dispersed. As he rapidly soared through the air, he even felt as though he were traveling between two oceans.

However, the deeper this sense of comfort became, the more the essence of his Tao began to surge, becoming difficult to suppress. Yet, Ji Yuan continued flying eastward, his path unwavering despite the vast expanse stretching before his eyes.

Before long, a suitable island came into view. With his supernatural eyes wide open, he surveyed it from the sky: apart from some vegetation, small beasts, and birds, the entire island was devoid of human habitation or large animals. It was the perfect place.

Calling it a “small island” was only in comparison to the mainland. In truth, it wasn’t actually all that small. It was at least large enough to accommodate a mountain range or perhaps a dozen peaks. Some were towering and steep, reaching heights of hundreds of zhang, their sharp, craggy peaks appearing almost like celestial creations.

Ji Yuan himself was still approaching on his cloud, his gaze sweeping over the island’s many peaks until he finally settled on one.

“Buzz…”

A soft hum sounded from the Green Vine Sword as it shot forth ahead of him, reaching the sky above the small island in the blink of an eye.

“Shiiing-“

The immortal sword flashed out of its sheath, its light glinting across one of the island’s many rocky peaks. A sharp section of the rock was directly sliced off of the summit of the highest peak. Immediately after, the sword whirled, the motion pulverizing the falling stone and turning it to dust, leaving only fine fragments to scatter with the wind.

Atop that stone peak, a flat, smooth surface was all that remained, about the size of a cushion. A few breaths later, Ji Yuan’s two feet landed squarely on top of it.

Ji Yuan glanced around; he was extremely satisfied with this location. After a brief moment of consideration, he changed out of his white robe and donned his gray robes. Then, he sat cross-legged right on the spot.

With eyes slightly closed, he concentrated his mind, inhaling and exhaling and circulating his energy within and without his body, and he began to cultivate and gain enlightenment.

The sun rose and set, the tides ebbed and flowed, winds and clouds shifted, the weather warmed and cooled…

Ji Yuan was not unaware of external events; on the contrary, he was acutely aware of the changes in the outside world. Whether it was lightning or thunder or a raging storm and rain, he perceived every shift. At night, when the sky was covered in a canopy of stars, he would even lift his head to look at them.

However, Ji Yuan seemed remarkably indifferent, showing no reaction to any changes. Whether wind or rain lashed at him or lightning shrouded him, he remained perfectly still. He was completely absorbed, his mind lost in thought, his spirit in a daze.

Gradually, the sensations from “A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds” faded from his mind but expanded throughout his body. The Five Elements within Ji Yuan surged and churned, resembling the changes in the weather he had observed — tempestuous clouds or gentle breezes and sunshine — the Five Elements circulated, mutually regenerating themselves and flourishing.

And when the energy of the Five Elements reached its peak, Ji Yuan gradually shifted from being influenced by the winds and clouds of the seasons, to instead influencing his surroundings. A sense of tranquility, like a gentle breeze and faint clouds, emanated from the mountain peak where Ji Yuan sat.

…

Although the country of Zuyue was at odds with the country of Dazhen, they generally used the same calendar system. This was a legacy dating back several dynasties, a remnant from the era of the Dazhou Dynasty, before it had fractured into many states.

Seasons shifted. By autumn of the year of Renchen,1 marking the fourth year of Ji Yuan’s secluded meditation on the island, a storm was brewing offshore.

This world had no weather forecasts, no global communication, and no one to issue storm warnings for ships at sea. Thus, vessels that ventured far out to sea sometimes faced unexpected severe weather, a test for both the ships and their crews.

Generally, fishing boats did not venture too far into the open ocean, partially due to inadequate preservation techniques for fish, partially due to the lack of live storage space,2 and also partially due to the sheer danger.

However, there were always exceptions. Many times, when there weren’t many fish to be caught near the coast, some large fishing vessels would bring a large crew of fishermen and head out to the distant seas together. Precious fish were kept in barrels and tanks, while those that couldn’t be kept alive were directly salted and stored.

Risk was proportional to reward, and a trip to the open ocean often yielded a bountiful harvest.

Since the beginning of summer this year, many areas near the coast of Qiangang3 Village and Pianwan4 Village had failed to yield many fish. Left with no choice, the two villages organized large fleets of boats, heading out to sea together to fish.

But this time, luck was not on their side, as the six large fishing vessels from Qiangang Village and Pianwan Village were caught in a sudden storm before they even had the chance to fill their holds with fish.

The sea churned with wind and rain, and the rumble of thunder made it impossible to hear shouts between the boats.

“Boom… crash-sh-shhh-shhh…”

Enormous waves slammed against the side of a fishing boat, jerking the fishermen aboard to and fro as they stumbled. The captain, clinging desperately to the rudder to steer the ship, was nearly swept away himself.

“What do we do? Are we going to die!?”

A young fisherman frantically shouted to the others around him. They looked around: some of the other boats were bobbing precariously, while others couldn’t be seen at all, perhaps having already sunk. One boat hadn’t even managed to lower its sails, and was in an extremely precarious situation.

“Shut up! Hold on tight!”

“Boom…”

“Aaah…”

A massive wave struck, sending the entire fishing boat careening to the side. One man was directly flung overboard, but those who had made it into the cabin clung desperately to the mast, railings, and other structures, with no strength left to save him.

“Hold on! Everyone hold on tight! Another big wave is coming–“

One man roared with all his might, but his voice was far too faint against the howling wind and rain and the surging waves of the sea.

“Boom… crash-sh-shhh-shhh…”

The boat lurched wildly, swaying from side to side, and only barely avoided capsizing entirely. All the items inside the cabin rolled and tumbled like dice rattling in a cup. Those hiding in the cabin not only got bruises all over their heads but also had to dodge various falling objects.

Humans were so insignificant in the face of such heavenly might, powerless as duckweed. Falling into the water offered almost no chance of survival.

The captains on the various vessels were the ones who bore the greatest pressure and faced the most danger. They had to preserve not only their own lives but also the lives of everyone on board.

A man who had tied himself to the railing repeatedly wiped the seawater from his face, when suddenly, he spotted an island. He immediately shouted frantically towards the captain of his ship.

“Captain Zhang— Captain Zhang—there’s an island over there!”

The captain, an elderly man with a grizzled beard and a sturdy build, trembled as he meticulously controlled the rudder. His eyes scanned in all directions as he strove to adjust the boat’s course before the next colossal wave arrived to avoid being capsized.

He had also seen the island in the distance, of course, but it had too few gentle slopes and too many steep cliff faces. If they tried to approach it now, the wind would blow them against the cliffs, crushing everyone to pieces. Moreover, even if they avoided the cliffs, in a place like this, the boats were extremely vulnerable to damage from hidden reefs, making survival equally difficult.

“Captain Zhang— let’s head for the island!”

Another fisherman roared. In such a crisis at sea, seeing a patch of land was like grasping at a lifeline.

“No—! Approaching it would be—it would just be seeking death! And besides, we can’t even get close…”

Atop the summit of one of the island’s peaks, Ji Yuan suddenly opened his eyes. A storm was raging, and the sky was dark. However, with his supernatural vision, he caught sight of multiple ships in the distance bobbing amidst the wind and rain, all visibly in imminent danger.

In recent years, Ji Yuan had rarely seen boats venture this far into the sea. After all, due to technological limitations, fishing boats couldn’t go far, and while cargo ships existed, most sailed along the coast or on specific routes.

Ji Yuan’s years of secluded meditation and enlightenment had yielded rich rewards. Though he had not yet finished, and ideally should not have been interrupted, it was not his way to stand by seeing so many ships in mortal danger before his eyes.

With a flick of his right sleeve, Ji Yuan drew in large amounts of the surrounding rainwater, gathering it all into a massive sphere of water in his palm. Then, Ji Yuan extended both hands into the water sphere, gently twisting and sliding them.

Ji Yuan mobilized his entire body’s spiritual energy with alongside his movements. His wind and water control techniques transformed the elements according to his will, and an edict lingered unspoken upon his tongue.

This was a great storm of the sea. Ji Yuan wanted to disperse it quickly, but even using wind and water control techniques to combat this vast expanse would consume a great deal of spiritual energy. Fortunately, this was not a celestial phenomenon, nor was it artificially driven by immortals or demons, so he could handle it with relative ease.

As Ji Yuan’s palms moved further and further apart, the sphere of water instantly expanded, expanding along with the space between his hands to form several massive, rippling characters made of water.

“Calm winds, scattered clouds!”

In the next moment, Ji Yuan sharply flicked his sleeve, sweeping the four large characters towards the sky.

Whoosh…

It was as if an invisible wave of spiritual essence had spread, and the surrounding storm gradually lost its momentum. The thick dark clouds in the sky began to slowly dissipate.

The large ships continued to sway left and right as the waves had not yet subsided, but those on board could clearly feel the instantaneous changes.

The waves grew smaller, and, most importantly, both the wind and rain had stopped.

Before long, the previous gloom gradually receded, and the clouds overhead slowly dispersed. Afternoon sunlight spilled down, warming the bewildered people on the boats.

Year 29 of the 60 year cycle

To keep fish fresh, fishermen can catch them alive and store them in barrels or tanks until they get to the fish market

前 (qián): front/ahead/first, 港 (gǎng): harbor/port

偏 (piān): to lean/to slant/to deviate, 湾 (wān): bay/gulf

Chapter 322: No Problem, No Problem At All!

“Haah… haah… haah… The wind, the storm, is it over?”

The captain, Old Man Zhang, lay panting on the rudder. The exhaustion was now hitting his body just like the storm that had just passed.

Many crewmembers stared blankly at the sea and sky, some even weeping tears of joy.

“The Gods have been merciful!1 The Gods have been merciful!”

“The heavens have saved us!”

“Perhaps the Dragon Lord of the sea has shown mercy!”

“We’re still alive! Hahahaha, we’re still alive—”

The people on the boat gradually came back to their senses and began to cheer and shout excitedly. The captain began to count the number of people remaining, while others searched for the other ships.

“Hey—over there, are you missing anyone? Have you seen the other ships?”

Someone on a nearby fishing vessel shouted, and Old Man Zhang quickly responded loudly.

“I see them… I’ve got three on my left, there’s one I haven’t seen yet!”

The surrounding sea was almost perfectly calm. The fishermen, having survived the ordeal, hurried either back below deck to tidy up, or to find their oars. They reached their oars through the oar holes to secure them and started rowing.

Several boats slowly converged. Fortunately, none of the six boats were missing; one had simply been blown too far by the waves and eventually drifted back.

However, not all the people on the six boats were accounted for: three individuals were missing. The minor scrapes and bruises sustained from collisions were, by comparison, small matters.

Although the fate of the three men was unknown, they had most likely perished at sea. However, even this outcome was already a blessing in disguise, compared to the crisis where they all might have been lost.

The survivors from the six boats conferred, and ultimately set up an altar on one of the center boats, placing offerings and an incense burner on it. Then, all the fishermen knelt on the deck, bowing to both the heavens and the sea.

Ji Yuan, from his peak on the distant island, gazed out at the six fishing boats. Although he couldn’t see what they were doing clearly with his physical sight, he could observe the auras of the rising incense power and spiritual energy. They were most likely offering offering sacrifices and giving thanks to the divine being who had rescued them.

Ji Yuan could even see some of the incense power drifting towards him. However, he merely glanced at it with his supernatural eyes and waved his hand, dispersing it. He had no intention of becoming entangled in the divine path.

The ships lingered nearby, searching for a while, but found no trace of the three missing individuals. They spent the night near the island before returning to the mainland the next day.

In the sky, the two previous Edicts had not yet completely dissipated.

Ji Yuan settled back into tranquility. With a wave of his hand, the colossal characters made of water vapor, which had expanded to a hundred zhang in diameter, seemed to be drawn back towards him. The invisible characters behaved like tangible objects, crashing onto the island with a rumbling roar, causing the mountain trees to shake for a moment before vanishing without a trace. Afterwards, Ji Yuan resumed his meditation.

It was only now that Ji Yuan truly understood the profound significance of the Qi of the Five Elements transforming into Yin and Yang. This concept was only spoken of in vague terms in “Bright Strategies” and “A Historical Account from Outside the Tao.” It was possible that not even their authors grasped this profound mystery.

Within Ji Yuan’s body, the Five Elements’ primordial Qi overflowed, circulating both within his body and around him. The connection between his inner world and the outer universe grew even closer, as if a new Golden Bridge of Heaven and Earth had been erected. He suddenly grew more sensitive, attuned to the shifting changes of the five elements in the external world.

The rocks, trees, soil, and surrounding seawater on the island combined with the Qi of the five elements, creating a unique symbiosis between heaven and earth.

It was no exaggeration to say that Ji Yuan could now deepen his understanding and easily wield any supernatural technique or divine ability, so long as it didn’t transcend the Five Elements. Similarly, he could now perceive even subtle environmental changes caused by others’ application of spiritual techniques with greater clarity.

Ji Yuan was unsure whether the current cultivators of this world, even those who claimed to have achieved the state of “Returning to Origin” through the cultivation of their Qi, could even have experienced anything similar.

Even though they might dare to claim that they successfully cultivated the combined Five Elements Qi, that didn’t mean their cultivation of this technique was complete. This was because the cycle of mutual generation was not yet fully established: even a slight discrepancy could lead to a huge error. Therefore, they likely couldn’t comprehend the profound wonder Ji Yuan was now experiencing.

Although “Bright Strategies” had once mocked the contemporary trend of cultivators claiming to have reached the “Returning to Origin” realm upon cultivating a single elemental Qi, it hadn’t gone into detail. Ji Yuan now thoroughly agreed with its sentiment. One could only say that the so-called “Returning to Origin” realm in the world of immortal cultivation and the true “Five Elements Returning to Origin” realm were two entirely different states.

Before this, Ji Yuan’s Five Elements’ Qi had already been quite active, but hadn’t yet reached its full potential. While “A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds” was meant to convey a carefree journey wandering freely across the universe, the experience and observations contained within it all embodied the transformations of the Five Elements. This had given Ji Yuan a significant push, allowing him to achieve complete cultivation in the Returning to Origin realm, and furthermore had a deeper, more profound influence on his inner world—though the latter was a matter of mental enlightenment, requiring no further meditative cultivation.

It wasn’t until the summer of the following year that Ji Yuan ended his secluded cultivation. He opened his eyes and gently exhaled. Faint strands of golden light emanated from his breath, the energy stretching far and wide.

After five years, Ji Yuan finally rose from the mountain peak. For a brief moment, his eyes shone with divine light, sharply defined at the corners, his pupils square-shaped. His originally gray eyes also took on a faint emerald sheen at the irises, but this change rapidly faded as Ji Yuan squinted.

“Green eyes, square pupils?”

Ji Yuan murmured to himself, recalling some descriptions of ancient immortals he had stumbled across in his previous life. Unlike the common portrayal of immortals popularized in film and literature, early ancient immortals were often described as having green eyes and square pupils.2

The Ji Yuan of his previous life, of course, did not understand at all why this would be the case, but now he could grasp the reason: the eyes, it was said, were an outward manifestation of the liver, and the liver was associated with the Wood element, connecting back to the pupils. With the completion of the Five Elements Qi and abundant spiritual energy, the element naturally manifested itself in his eyes as green irises and square pupils. To ordinary folk, it might even seem uncanny, but in truth, it was one of the hallmarks of high accomplishment in immortal cultivation.

Ji Yuan calculated the time and realized that the year of Renchen was almost over. However, it wasn’t that too much time had passed, but rather too little. For an ordinary cultivator to reach his current realm, it would have taken countless years of arduous cultivation, often fraught with calamities, tribulations, and bottlenecks. Yet, by his own estimation, Ji Yuan had only been cultivating for less than twenty years.

“A Dream of Wandering in the Clouds has helped me greatly. If I ever meet Zhong Pingxiu in the future, I must certainly repay him!”

True Immortals, of course, were not truly immortal in the sense of eternal life, but rather in that their lifespan was greatly increased, so it was very likely he was still alive.

With that thought, Ji Yuan glanced at the open sea, then turned and leaped into the air, steppingon the clear wind as he flew westward.

…

Qiangang Village and Pianwan Village were among the many fishing villages bordering the Eastern Sea. The two villages were quite close, and intermarriage was common, making them relatively intimate communities.

As the saying went, “those who live by the mountains eat of the mountains, and those who live by the sea eat of the sea.” Normally, fishermen sustained themselves by fishing in the coastal waters.

However, in the past year or two, catches in the coastal waters had become exceptionally scarce. The people of both villages, and even of the other coastal fishing villages nearby, were forced to venture frequently into more distant waters to make ends meet.

The waters were dangerous, and voyages took a long time. The fishing fleets from Qiangang Village and Pianwan Village had once been caught in a storm that almost led to their ships being wrecked and everyone perishing.

However, at that time, the six large fishing boats from the two villages miraculously weathered the storm and survived. Gradually, boats from further out at sea began to learn of a miraculous island.

No matter the weather, the winds and rains near that island would always be significantly milder. Even if they suddenly encountered a storm, as long as they could take shelter near the island in time, their chances of surviving would greatly increase.

The fishermen were willing to believe that the island was being protected by a deity, calling it Dingfeng3 Island. Whenever fishing boats approached the island, they would set up an altar on board to offer sacrifices and prayers for blessings.

However, even with Dingfeng Island serving as a blessed haven for fishing boats, as all the vessels from the surrounding coast rushed there to fish, the fish around the island would eventually be depleted, forcing them to venture back into more dangerous waters.

This situation was unsustainable in the long run. The most crucial factor was the continued lack of fish in the vast coastal areas. Consequently, at the turn of this new year, several surrounding fishing villages came together and decided to pay a hefty price.

It was on this very day that Ji Yuan, riding the wind, returned to the mainland.

As he flew through the sky, Ji Yuan habitually scanned the nearby villages. He happened to spot a village below bustling with the sounds of drumming and the clanging of hammers. It was a very lively sight. A large crowd had gathered, far exceeding the population of the entire village, and people were continuously arriving from outside the village.

“Is this some New Year’s custom I haven’t seen before?’

With this thought, Ji Yuan descended from the sky. It had been a long time since he’d joined in such a lively atmosphere; it would be good to experience it, and perhaps even take part in a celebratory meal. He had done such things many times in the past.

Ji Yuan, dressed in a grey robe, landed behind a hill. Seeing a young fisherman in a cotton padded jacket pass by, rubbing his hands from the cold, he emerged and pretended to be merely passing through as well.

“Hey, young man, please wait a moment!”

At this call, the young man with a childish face really did stop. In Ji Yuan’s experience, youngsters from the countryside tended to be pure-hearted, naturally less wary, and sometimes more impulsive, but also more willing to help when earnestly asked.

The young man looked at Ji Yuan, who was dressed in his wide-sleeved long robe. He looked to be a scholar, and spoke quite politely.

“Sir, were you calling me?”

“Ah, yes, that’s right, young man. My name is Ji Yuan. I have traveled here from qutie some distance and got a little lost when I arrived. I see many people heading towards that village, and it sounds quite lively with all the drumming and hammering. May I ask if this is an important New Year’s custom here?”

Ji Yuan asked politely, bowing with cupped hands in greeting as he approached the young man.

The young man hurriedly returned a somewhat awkward cupped-hand bow.

“Over there? Oh, it’s not really a New Year’s custom. New Year’s Eve is in a few days, a time to bid farewell to the old and welcome the new. Several villages pooled their money to invite a great Taoist priest to exorcise evil spirits and ensure that the coastal waters will be bountiful with fish next year. This is by the Taoist master’s request: he said there must be a strong human presence. Many people from the surrounding fishing villages have come. And tomorrow, there will be a banquet large enough for a thousand people and a lantern display!”

“Oh, oh, oh… That is quite unusual…”

Ji Yuan responded, suddenly understanding. The last portion of his words was a low murmur as he was deep in thought. He looked towards the village. Just these few sentences allowed him to deduce a wealth of information, such as the scarcity of fish along the coast.

However, his thoughtful expression was fleeting. Ji Yuan quickly put on a distressed look.

“I had thought it was a New Year’s celebration and intended to brazenly join in the fun. Never did I imagine it was an exorcism! Ah, they surely won’t allow an outsider like me to participate. I’ve been separated from my friends and have been wandering alone in the countryside for quite some time now, feeling both hungry and exhausted…”

The young man at his side scratched his head, then hesitantly spoke.

“Actually, the more people who can come for the exorcism, the better. If you don’t mind potentially being contaminated by the evil spirits, sir, then surely would be no problem for you to join us for the thousand-person banquet, would it?”

Ji Yuan’s face lit up with pleasant surprise, and took a few steps closer.

“Not a problem, not a problem at all! It’s been so long since I’ve eaten, why would I be so reserved? Come on, come on, little brother, let’s go together! If anyone asks, please cover for me!”

Chapter 323: Never Underestimate the People of this World

Seeing Ji Yuan’s overly familiar demeanor, the young man, on the other hand, grew a bit reserved. After all, the person before him was clearly a scholar, and was even wearing a jade hairpin in his hair.

“Then please, sir, come along with me. There’s truly nothing to worry about. Though times are hard these days, the nearby fishing villages are still quite hospitable, especially since you’re a scholar, right? ”

Ji Yuan nodded in agreement as he fell into step with the young man.

“Naturally, I am educated, and I’m relieved to hear that. By the way, I haven’t yet had the honor of asking your name, young friend? ”

Ji Yuan’s very forward inquiry made the young man feel a little awkward, and he scratched his head again as he replied.

“Sir, there’s no need to be so polite. My name is nothing special, I’m Liang Pingle.”1

“Ah, Young Brother Liang, ‘peace and happiness’—your elders have certainly given you a name with excellent meaning! ”

Ji Yuan said with a smile, and Liang Pingle smiled in return. His grandfather had given him this name, and it was quite gratifying for a learned scholar to recognize it.

After this brief exchange, Ji Yuan had gained some understanding of Liang Pingle, and his gaze now turned towards the distant village.

“Young Brother Liang, would you be so kind as to tell me what kind of evil spirits you are trying to exorcise? I have traveled to many places and enjoy hearing such strange tales.”

Liang Pingle gradually relaxed as he conversed with Ji Yuan as they walked, even feeling honored to converse with a man of learning. Hearing Ji Yuan’s question, and knowing it wasn’t anything secret, he told him everything.

“Sir, you wouldn’t know, but our villages here by the sea are all fishing villages, specifically coastal fishermen. One of the advantages is that the taxes aren’t as heavy as they would be for farmers, so our lives as coastal fishermen were quite decent. However, since the end of last year, it’s become very difficult to get good catches in the waters near the shore.”

“How so? Could it be that the fish have all been caught? ”

Ji Yuan asked casually.

“The sea is boundless! There are countless fish, shrimp, and crabs in the ocean, how could they possibly all have been caught?2 At most, there might be fewer fish for a while if the tides are a little off, but there is no way we wouldn’t be able to catch any fish at all.”

“That’s true. ”

Ji Yuan concurred. Given the skills and population of these fishing villages, it was naturally impossible for them to deplete the resources of an entire ocean.

“So, you suspect evil spirits? ”

“Sir, you’ve hit the nail on the head! All the folks in the villages along the coast feel the same way. They’ve long considered inviting Taoist priests to take a look, but several priests have come and none of them could find anything wrong. We’ve offered sacrifices to Heaven, the land, the gods, and the spirits, but nothing has worked.”

Ji Yuan did not stop walking, but turned his head to look at Liang Pingle.

“Since you know it isn’t going to work, why are you going to such lengths again? ”

“Well, the problem still hasn’t been solved, has it? And the authorities aren’t doing anything. So the elders from several villages put their heads together and wondered if the priests they’d invited simply weren’t powerful enough. Even though we have Dingfeng Island, it’s still a long journey. Now, with the end of the year approaching, everyone gritted their teeth, and every village, every family, pooled their money to hire the most powerful Taoist priest. His magic is absolutely incomparable to any of the previous ones! ”

Ji Yuan nodded, feigning a slight surprise on his face.

“Oh, I see! Gathering the energy of the amassed people to boost human vitality—this priest certainly has some real skill. ”

“Hey, sir, you understand this, too?”

“I’ve been to many places and seen many similar spectacles. The liveliest of such events I’ve been to had hundreds, even thousands of experts jumping about together; it was quite a sight. ”

Ji Yuan recalled the Water and Land Assembly from way back then, and casually answered.

“Oh, is that so! ”

Liang Pingle imagined the scene Ji Yuan described and thought it must indeed have been truly lively. Just then, footsteps rapidly approached, and several burly men walked in from a converging side road, greeting Liang Pingle when they saw him.

“Hey, Xiao Liang, you’ve come too! Who’s this gentleman beside you? I haven’t seen him before! ”

“Brother-in-law! Is it true that every household in your village sent a man over here? I wanted to come too; my father will arrive a little later. Oh, right, this is the esteemed gentleman I just met; he’s a scholar from afar and has seen similar events before.”

Ji Yuan cupped his hands in greeting towards the men.

“My name is Ji Yuan. I am a wanderer who has just arrived here and would like to observe the ritual here with you. ”

The identity of a scholar was indeed very useful, especially someone like Ji Yuan, who was poised and of extraordinary demeanor. His appearance did not easily indicate his age, but he was certainly not young.

Those nearby didn’t dare to be negligent in their manners and quickly returned the greeting, once again inviting Ji Yuan to come along.

This wasn’t some shameful affair; moreover, the priest had even said that having someone with an official or scholarly aura in attendance would be even better.

The man who greeted Liang Pingle was named Zhang Fu. He was far more talkative than Liang Pingle. Before, it had been Ji Yuan asking questions and Liang Pingle answering, but now that it was Zhang Fu’s turn, Ji Yuan didn’t even need to say anything; he just started talking non-stop.

He even clearly explained about Dingfeng Island, which Ji Yuan had wanted to ask about. Just as he had thought, it was indeed the small island where he had previously been cultivating. The island was originally called Shanjianzi Island,3 but now the surrounding fishermen all called it Dingfeng Island.

They did not have to continue journeying far, and soon, the group entered Pianwan Village together. In front of the village, a large area of sandy ground had been cleared. Numerous gongs and drums had been placed inside the cleared area, alongside several piles of firewood, presumably for a large bonfire to be lit that night.

From a distance, although his vision was blurry, Ji Yuan still make out many people circling around on the sandy area, periodically shouting a few phrases along with the leader. Each time, the gongs and drums would sound out loudly.

“All fellow villagers, from far and wide~! Gather here to raise the yang energy~!”

“Raise the yang energy~!”

“Raise the yang energy~!”

. . .

Ji Yuan stared blankly for a moment, then turned and saw Liang Pingle and Zhang Fu receiving several strips of red ribbon from a village woman nearby.

“Uh, is there any special meaning to what they’re doing? ”

Zhang Fu handed a strip of red ribbon to the bewildered Ji Yuan.

“Sir, the mage said that tying this red ribbon to your arm will ward off malevolent spirits. Everyone over there is wearing them, even on the sandy arena. They are currently rousing the Yang Fire. ”

“Rousing the Yang Fire? Is this also something the expert mentioned? ”

This was a new term, one Ji Yuan hadn’t heard before.

“Yes, these two days are for rousing the Yang Fire. Tomorrow, after the thousand-person banquet, starting at dusk we’ll light a lantern formation. A single long line of torches will drive away evil spirits along the coast, and that way, we can drive out the evil spirit that’s scaring the fish! In a month or so, the fish will return! ”

Zhang Fu said, a hint of excitement still on his face.

Ji Yuan wore a look of sudden realization, nodding in agreement with the others’ enthusiasm.

“Oh, oh… I’ve learned something new! So that’s how it is! ”

“Haha, sir, although you’re a scholar, you probably don’t know much about these kinds of things. The one we’ve invited is a truly formidable expert, renowned in the Tao on the eastern continent. There’s no evil spirit he can’t subdue! ”

Ji Yuan couldn’t help but laugh.

“Then I certainly must make his acquaintance! ”

“Enough talk, enough talk! Sir, quickly tie on the red ribbon on your and come with us to rouse the Yang Fire! The more people, the better! ”

Ji Yuan looked at the red ribbon in his hand, then at Zhang Fu, Liang Pingle, and the others, who already had theirs tied to their arms.

“Do I have to tie it too? Since this red cloth wards off evil, I should be fine just holding it, shouldn’t I? ”

“Uh, the expert didn’t say anything about that. ”

“If he didn’t say it’s not allowed, then it’s allowed. I will just hold it then. By the way, the expert is over there, right? ”

Ji Yuan quickly settled the matter.

“Of course he is. ”

“Then let’s go quickly! ”

If Ji Yuan wanted to partake in their thousand-person banquet, he naturally had to follow local customs. Besides, this whole event was quite interesting; he might as well treat it as entertainment. So, he followed Zhang Fu and the others to the sandy ground and quickly disappeared into the crowd.

However, Ji Yuan was simply strolling along, casually observing, not joining in the shouting. As the group approached the firewood piles, he saw the renowned Taost priest

He wore a large, wide robe, red and yellow in color, adorned with Yin-Yang symbols, the Eight Trigrams, and various cloud patterns. On his head, he wore a tall hairpin crown embellished with luminous pearls. A precious sword was strapped to his back, and he wielded a horsetail whisk, swinging it back and forth. He certainly looked quite impressive.

Somewhat unexpectedly, to Ji Yuan’s surprise, this Taoist actually possessed a genuine flow of spiritual energy and Qi throughout his body. However, this was the so-called ‘mixed flow spiritual energy,’ meaning it wasn’t power refined through orthodox methods, but rather refined through meditation and visualizing the inner fire.

Ji Yuan’s bearing and attire stood out remarkably among the fishermen, drawing a lot fo attention. Many fishermen were staring at him; even the Taoist had noticed Ji Yuan.

Seeing Ji Yuan smile and nod towards him, the Taoist priest paused for a moment. Though his whisk didn’t stop swinging, he also nodded back at Ji Yuan in acknowledgment.

This ‘rousing of Yang Fire’ continued until nightfall. Fortunately, it wasn’t a continuous event; Ji Yuan merely walked a few laps before emerging with Zhang Fu and the others.

Bonfires had been lit on the beach, and the villagers who had traveled here from the surrounding area to participate in the ‘rousing of Yang Fire’ dispersed and returned home, awaiting the thousand-person banquet and lantern formation tomorrow evening.

Zhang Fu, Liang Pingle, and Liang’s father, who arrived later in the evening, all stayed. Zhang Fu was originally from Pianwan Village; he had gone out to recruit people from other villages. Since the Liang family and Zhang family were in-laws, he didn’t need to return today. Consequently, Ji Yuan naturally stayed with the Zhang family as well.

Dinner consisted of taro rice,4 steamed salted fish, and a bowl of vegetable soup. Both families were afraid that Ji Yuan, being a scholar, wouldn’t be used to the food, so they asked several times about his dietary restrictions while preparing the meal. Only when they saw Ji Yuan eating heartily at the dinner table did they feel relieved.

Fishing villages like Pianwan Village and the surrounding towns hadn’t produced a single scholar in over a decade or two. Currently, the village head could barely read a few characters, far inferior compared to Ji Yuan. Both families treated Ji Yuan as a distinguished guest.

In the middle of the night, Ji Yuan left the fishing village alone and went to the shore. Scanning the tides, he didn’t detect any demonic auras, at least not in the vicinity of Pianwan Village. However, the sea was too mysterious to be certain. He planned to investigate thoroughly after this farce had concluded, preferably without entering the water.

The thousand-person banquet on the next day was exceptionally lively. People from various villages brought gifts to Pianwan Village. This matter didn’t just affect one village or one place; everyone worked together to prepare the banquet. Plus, it was around the time of the New Year, so the atmosphere was festive.

While eating, Ji Yuan discovered that the residents of Pianwan Village began to grow unprecedentedly agitated, especially when nearly a thousand men lined up to receive their torches. Their collective energy reached its peak.

Ji Yuan suddenly realized that the Taoist priest they had invited was indeed skilled; this change was not due to the priest’s own spiritual energy, but rather a kind of psychological manipulation or suggestion.

After so many ritualistic processes, and with a thousand male villagers well-fed and emboldened, coupled with the long line of torches, the crowd developed a formidable surge of energy, the aura similar to that of a military force.

Ji Yuan also received a torch and joined the procession. The Taoist, similarly holding a torch, was at the very front. At this moment, his voice boomed like thunder, ringing out in a mighty roar:

“Everyone, prepare to follow me! Get in formation! The long line begins!”

The next moment, the Taoist, lantern in hand, began to move, leading the way from Pianwan Village and advancing along the coastline. A long snaking line of torches, carrying the surging human vitality, flowed along the seashore.

Ji Yuan watched this scene from the middle of the procession and muttered to himself.

“How interesting! Indeed, one must never underestimate the people of this world!”

Surname Liang, 平 (píng): calm/peaceful, 乐 (lè): happy/cheerful

T/n: a moment of silence for modern day overfishing practices… That being said, it probably wouldn’t be a concern in Ancient China.

Chapter 324: Collapsing At the First Blow

[T/n: note on chapter title]1

Ji Yuan now somewhat believed what Zhang Fu had said earlier. Judging by this current situation, it was indeed possible to scare away some weaker demons, and perhaps even some demons with a small measure of power, as this formation truly seemed to have been arranged by a master.

“Demons, leave this place! Shout with me! Demons, leave this place!”

The expert at the front roared, and the villagers behind him followed, shouting in unison.

“Demons, leave this place!”

“Demons, leave this place!”

At first, their voices were disorganized, but soon they grew increasingly unified, their shouts seemingly influenced by the long line of torches in formation. They were exceptionally aloud, advancing forward as echoing along as the formation array of torches stretched out over three hundred zhang.

The surroundings had originally been pitch black, but now they were completely illuminated by the torchlight. Being coastal fishermen, they were very careful of their footing along the shore, ensuring they wouldn’t accidentally fall into the sea. Under the expert’s guidance, whenever a large wave crashed into the shore, the villagers’ shouts would grow even louder.

“Demons, leave this place!”

“Demons, leave this place!”

Although the torchlight seemed to only extend out so far, in Ji Yuan’s supernatural eyes, the human vital energy had coalesced into a rolling formation, constantly spreading and affecting more and more of the nearby area, as if suppressing the very essence of the surrounding ocean. The oppressive aura intensified.

“Blub blub blub…”

On the surface of the distant sea, bubbled surged in waves. It wasn’t very noticeably due to the interference of the waves, but underwater, the currents on the seabed were already churning, creating a spot of murky water.

“Crash, crash…”

The waves ahead grew larger and later, but the expert at the front was not afraid. He did not cower — instead, he raised his torch and ran directly towards the nearby shore, the long line of villagers following behind him.

“Thousands of households’ vital flames surge forth; humans, gods, ghosts, and demons, none shall infringe upon any other; treading on heaven and earth to send forth this dragon of fire, surging celestial Yang energy sweeping forth. If any aquatic spirit dwells on this shore, flee quickly—”

The Taoist shouted with all his might, the waves of his voice instantly spreading throughout the entire procession.

He had already investigated the entire coastline and learned the details of this coastal area over the past year or so. The fishermen had never been attacked by monsters, nor had they encountered any monsters when they went out to sea; at most, there was simply no fish.

This definitely didn’t seem like the work of some powerful demon. It was more likely that some aquatic creature had gained spiritual enlightenment and cultivated a consciousness, and was roaming around nearby, scaring off the fish. For this kind of being, it sufficed to use a torch formation and human vital energy to assist in the creation of a grand array based on the fire of human vitality. Any inexperienced minor demon would be terrified!

The shoreline of this coast was all rock, but the water below was extremely deep. Ordinary areas of shallow water could never sustain spiritual creatures or demonic beings, so it could only be here.

“Leave this place!”

“Leave this place!”

All the villagers roared in unison. To mortal eyes, waves were simply surging and crashing against the shore, but in Ji Yuan’s supernatural vision, a dragon formed of the fire of human vitality had actually swept into the sea, stirring up the waters even more ferociously than the waves.

As the crowd held their torches and formed the formation, their courage soared to unprecedented heights. More importantly, everyone was willing to believe the in the Taoist priest’s immense power. For an instant, a surging celestial Yang energy erupted, like a massive wave crashing towards the ocean surface, and even threatening to sweep across the shoreline.

At that moment.

“Whoosh! Whoosh! Whoosh!”

A great wave suddenly rose wildly from the sea, with a momentum far exceeding what it could have achieved with the wind on this night, crashing towards the group at the front of the line. By the time the villagers saw it in the torchlight, the great wave was already upon them.

“Oh no! Get out of the way—”

The expert at the front cried out, rushing to the very front of the enormous wave. The horsetail whisk in his hand spun wildly as he circulated the spiritual energy within his body to try and resist, but the moment the very tip of his spiritual energy made contact, he felt a devastating blow, and his entire body was wracked with unbearable pain.

The great wave struck so suddenly that the crowd, in their confusion, had no time to dodge.

“Boom—”

The giant wave smashed into the front of the torch formation, seemingly hitting a transparent sphere. Countless crashes of water exploded all around, surging upwards and downwards. Many villagers stared blankly at the sky above, as if witnessing the a heavenly river in its full glory.

“Crash-shhh-shhh-shhh…”

A large amount of water and ocean spray fell onto the beach behind them. Many villagers were still caught in between bewilderment and fright, but at this scene, their hearts were suddenly filled with excitement.

“Leave this place!”

“Leave this place!”

Many villagers continued to shout after regaining their senses, screaming their throats raw in fervent excitement.

In the procession, the Taoist priest, still holding his horsetail whisk, clutched at his furiously beating chest, his eyes suspiciously scanning back and forth along the procession.

He knew that he absolutely could not have blocked that wave just now with his own power; even the torch formation would have been directly shattered.

Moreover, when that great wave struck, it carried a strong, fishy stench, clearly indicating something powerful had created this wall of water. The expert had instinctively wanted to block it, but he regretted it the moment the great wave collided with his whisk’s spiritual energy and divine light.

The cheering and shouting fishermen had no idea of the danger that had just passed, but as the expert directly involved, he had been scared half to death.

‘Just which expert is assisting me?’

“Master, are we not moving forward anymore?”

The villagers’ shouts gradually subsided. One of the village chiefs, seeing the expert staring blankly around, unmoving, couldn’t help but shout a question.

The Taoist came to his senses and flicked his whisk.

“No need to move. Just stand here. Everyone come closer, stomp your feet and shout towards the sea, keep your steps in unison!”

The expert no longer sought out the master who was assisting him; either way, since the other party was helping, it was now time for all of them to redouble their efforts.

Over a thousand people gathered together. Some torches were about to burn out, so they immediately lit their spare ones. More and more people stomped their feet alongside the expert.

“Thump thump. . . thump. . . thump.”

With over a thousand people stomping in rhythm, even if each person’s footsteps was only just a tiny sound, together they created a powerful momentum. Combined with the scene of the large wave having been broken, everyone’s spirits soared.

Ji Yuan’s immediately thought back to the “Song of the Hand-Forged Blade”2 and other common methods the military used to boost morale.

“Rooooar!”

A roar echoed from the water. Bubbles surged on the ocean surface, accompanied by a strong, fishy stench. A mass of seawater rose from the ocean floor, streams cascading down from above to reveal a massive, jet-black spine.

“Crash-shhh-shhh-shhh…”

Amidst the continuous sound of flowing water, the stomping sounds petered out. The villagers fell silent. Some instinctively backed away, including the Taoist priest.

“M-monster? Master, a monster has appeared!”

“Master! Master, there really is a monster!”

“Is that a giant whale? “

“It seems so!”

“M-master, what do we do? How do we exorcise the demon?”

“Where’s the master?”

“Master!”

“Master, quickly come to the front!”

In their panic, some fishermen pushed the Taoist priest, who had retreated to the back, to the very front.

Rising from the sea was an enormous whale, much larger than any whale the fishermen had ever encountered. To those nearby, it was practically a small hill.

“Gulp.”

The master gulped hard, and stood trembling with fear at the very front, facing this great whale that seemed capable of crushing him to death with a single blow.

“Y-you, what domain do you hold, demon? This coast is the livelihood of the surrounding fishermen. You’re driving away all the fish here, making it impossible for thousands of coastal fishermen to survive. Do you know how a great sin this is?”

Seeing that the sea monster didn’t react, the sorcerer forced himself to gather his courage and loudly rebuked it. However, his tone was rather gentle, and his eyes kept darting around.

There must be a powerful master present tonight. He hoped that master would see things through from beginning to end and help him. Hopefully, they hadn’t left yet.

One of the giant whale’s eyes turned to look at the Taoist priest. That gaze made the priest’s heart tremble, and his muscles tensed.

“Scram—”

“Boom!”

A tremendous sound suddenly exploded. Endless waves erupted around the giant whale. The water surged skyward, then fell back down to the ground , crashing like heavy rain, soaking many of the villagers’ torches.

For a moment, everyone retreated in disarray, disheveled, covered in dust and mud. The Taoist priest even collapsed directly on the ground.

The giant whale glanced contemptuously at the priest on the ground by the shore, its gaze sweeping back and forth over the cowering fishermen. The once-magnificent torch formation had long since been broken, and even the human vital fire energy in the crowd now seemed to have dispersed.

“Where is the great master? May I see you?”

The entire procession remained silent, looking around before finally turning to the Taoist priest, who had now stood back up. However, the latter quickly shrank to the back, not daring to get too close to the shore again.

“Can you reveal yourself?”

The giant whale’s voice rang out again, still as loud as before, and its body moved even closer to the shore, frightening the villagers into continuous retreat. However, Ji Yuan did not reveal himself.

Although this monster’s aura was powerful, there was no killing intent.

“I am General Giant Whale. Since the master will not show himself, these fishermen have offended me, so I shall slaughter them all! Rooooar!”

The giant whale let out a beast-like roar, its massive tail lifted high.

But at that moment, Ji Yuan had already gripped the Green Vine Sword in his hand. He unsheathed three inches of the blade and flicked it with his finger.

“Clang!”

An invisible Sword Qi emanated from the immortal sword, striking the giant whale with its tail raised.

“Whoosh!”

“Boom!”

The whale’s tail crashed back down, failing to churn any large waves. The giant whale tumbled away, appearing to be in extreme pain.

“Crash-shhh!”

Water flew everywhere as the whale directly parted the seawater, enveloped in a large amount of ocean spray, swimming rapidly downwards, headed for the open sea.

‘Heh. General Giant Whale.’

Ji Yuan chucked as he watched the whale disappear into the distance. Then, his figure faded away as he stepped on the waves and gave chase.

Meanwhile, the surrounding fishermen and the expert were still dazed and frightented.
