Chapter 333: Nothing Really Catches the Eye

An hour had passed by the time Ji Yuan and the Dragon Daughter had finished touring the interior of the ship and emerged. Outside, the excitement surrounding the arrival of the powerful figures aboard the flying ship had largely faded.

Now, apart from a few people still lingering by the railing of the ship, gazing at the motionless giant whale on the sea surface below, most had returned to their normal activities, going about their business.

Thus, when Chi Gui led Ji Yuan and the Dragon Daughter to the market, only a few people who either possessed exceptionally keen eyesight or recognized Chi Gui and Lin Jian could infer that they were being personally escorted. Most were largely unaware that Ji Yuan and Ying Ruoli were the individuals from the giant whale.

Unlike the relatively spacious interior of the ship, as Ji Yuan and the Dragon Daughter followed behind Chi Gui and Lin Jian, they saw what appeared to be a long street on the deck, lined on both sides with building of varying heights, some single-story, some two or three stories.

Most of these buildings seemed to be shops of some kind, with signs hanging out front. Many were even operated by ordinary people, selling food or daily necessities.

Of course, there were also those selling “extraordinary items.” Some were still in the form of shops, while others had simply found a spot for themselves in the building and laid out their wares as if it were a stall.

As Ji Yuan walked along, he took a leisurely look around, finding it all quite novel. This was the first time in his life he had seen so many cultivators gathered together in such a small area. Although their cultivation levels varied, most were proper immortal cultivators, and there were certainly quite a few of them.

Although the atmosphere during the Water and Land Assembly of Emperor Yuan De of Dazhen was chaotic, and many cultivators from Yuhuai Mountain had arrived, they were in fact dispersed throughout the capital and not particularly numerous. Furthermore, that group of demons was quickly wiped out, so the experience hadn’t left a deep impression. It was nowhere near as lively as this “gathering of cultivators.”

“Haha, Mister Ji, this realm-crossing ferry—aside from helping people traverse realms, sometimes also serves as a mobile market for cultivators. When it docks, there’s no shortage of visitors, be they immortal cultivators with shallower foundations or those seeking convenience, be they demon cultivators, ghost cultivators, and even divine beings.”

Ji Yuan nodded in deep agreement as he listened to Chi Gui’s words.

Lin Jian also chimed in from the side.

“Cultivators are also sentient beings; they too possess the seven emotions and six desires. Compared to mortals, their desires are less intense, but they still have things they want and realms they aspire to. Mortals believe immortal cultivators are without desires, but it’s just that they have little interest in worldly matters! If a gold nugget were to fall to the ground, a mortal would pick it up, while we would regard it as a mere weed. But if a mountain spirit stone were to drop to the ground, a mortal would think it but a pebble, while we would regard it as a treasure.”

Ji Yuan chuckled, and half-jokingly spoke.

“If I saw a gold nugget, I’d pick it up too. “

The Dragon Daughter beside him almost burst out laughing. From what she had heard from her father and based on her own understanding, she knew very well that Uncle Ji acted as he pleased, free and unrestrained, and also enjoyed exploring the mortal world. If he said he would pick it up, he really would do so.

Chi Gui and Lin Jian took his words as a joke and continued leading the way.

In the center of the street ahead stood a colossal mast. This towering wooden pillar was one of the most common places where cultivators came to exchange goods.

“Let’s go, I’ll go inside and take a look! “

On a whim, Ji Yuan strode over, looked around, found a building with a sign that read “Transformation Pavilion,” and entered.

It was quiet inside. Even the occasional conversation wasn’t loud; immortal cultivators setting up stalls wouldn’t hawk loudly.

There weren’t many people inside, nor were there too many stalls. Behind each small table sat several cultivators dressed in a variety of styles. Ji Yuan and his three companions naturally attracted attention, but but no one called out to solicit customers.

On the small tables in front of them lay items that could be considered “goods,” including spiritual talismans and rare artifacts. Ji Yuan was quite surprised to see two “bonsai.”

Curious, he stepped closer and examined them intently. The two pots contained the same type of plant, resembling a thick vine that spiraled upwards, about a foot tall, with small leaves bearing long, sharp thorns. Both the leaves and thorns were purple.

Seeing Ji Yuan stop to look, the cultivator sitting cross-legged and eyes closed behind the small table opened his eyes and offered a brief introduction.

“This is a Purple Thorn Vine. The pot is an ordinary clay pot, and the soil is black soil soaked in spiritual spring water. It can ensure the Purple Thorn Vine does not die. If carefully cared for, it might flower and bear fruit in the future, which can stabilize the soul and replenish vital energy. “

“Oh, how long would it take to flower and bear fruit?”

Ji Yuan casually asked. The cultivator smiled.

“This depends on personal fate. The Purple Thorn Vine is quite rare; if it doesn’t reach maturity, it’s hard for it to nurture a spirit. If it does, it constantly tries to escape, making it difficult to control. I’ve cared for these two Purple Thorn Vines for thirty years, and they still haven’t flowered or borne fruit, I might as well trade them for something else. “

Ji Yuan replied, “You’re quite honest! “

He then shook his head, no longer looking at the two Purple Thorn Vines, and walked elsewhere. The cultivator didn’t try to stop him, closing his eyes again to meditate.

The higher a cultivator’s level, the firmer their resolve. When it came to buying things, their wants and dislikes were clear as black and white. They would naturally acquire what they wanted, and it was useless to convince them otherwise if they didn’t want something. Therefore, aside from special circumstances, it was rare to see shopkeepers making flowery promises about their wares while the customer hesitated.

After casually browsing through the building, Ji Yuan found that the items for sale didn’t contain anything he particularly wanted. Or rather, he was generally interested in almost everything, but only out of curiosity.

Since that was the case, and since he had no suitable “currency,” he naturally wouldn’t buy anything. He was content to just look around.

After browsing a few more so-called immortal shops, Ji Yuan’s interest had waned.

“Uncle Ji, is there anything you want to buy? If you don’t have suitable items for trade, I have some. I have hundreds of catties of Water Spirit Essence,1 and some Deep Sea Spirit Gathering Pearls too. “

“Hiss. . . “

Even Chi Gui and Lin Jian, though they were Returning to Origin-level cultivators, couldn’t help but subtly gasp. Listening to the Dragon Daughter’s casual attitude, it seemed these things were nothing to her.

Even Ji Yuan was secretly astonished. From this stroll, he had gained some understanding of the Five Element spirit stones and other such items. Take the Purple Thorn Vine he had just seen, for instance; it cost one catty of Water Spirit Essence, which looked like nothing more than an ice bead the size of a broad bean. The Dragon Daughter’s offer was practically a blatant display of wealth.

“Never mind, never mind. Though these things are rare and interesting, there’s nothing that truly catches my eye. Acquiring them would be useless. “

Ji Yuan was telling the truth. He had actually quite wanted to look for a Substitute Life Talisman, like the one he’d encountered before, but there weren’t any. As for the other items, from medicinal herbs to spiritual talismans and various so-called spiritual artifacts, they were all expendable to him.

As for magical artifacts, Ji Yuan felt that at present, an immortal sword, a wolf-hair brush, and a fishing rod were sufficient. As for items assisting cultivation, Five Element spirit stones could have been useful, but back then he hadn’t known they could be traded and collected this way. Now that he had achieved the Five Qi Converging to the Origin and the perfect fusion of the Five Elements, they weren’t really that useful anymore, unless used lavishly to aid in casting Five-Element techniques.

So, the problem was this: his immortal sword didn’t need such items; the wolf-hair brush was mostly used for casting Edicts, so these goods were also useless; lightning control used the power of the lightning talisman, so it didn’t need wood spiritual essence.

He could control water, but his skill, while meticulous, lacked the ability to manipulate towering waves. Using water spiritual essence would be too extravagant. He regrettably didn’t know any earth elemental techniques. And for fire control, his control of any other fire techniques was about as inept as his water control. Even if fire spiritual essence could enhance his True Fire of Samadhi, its consumption would be enormous.

Moreover, Ji Yuan was not proficient in alchemy, nor did he understand medicine; using those those spiritual herbs would just be squandering heavenly resources.

After much thought, Ji Yuan really couldn’t think of anything he urgently needed right now, at least not on this realm-crossing ferry from the Nine Peaks Mountain.

At that thought, Ji Yuan sensed the aroma of food wafting from some shops up ahead. He pointed and strode towards them.

“Come, let’s find a good restaurant, order a table full of dishes, and have a good meal. I’ve barely eaten anything worth mentioning these past few years. “

Chi Gui and Lin Jian exchanged glances, feeling they should still follow.

Meanwhile, the Dragon Daughter, accompanying them, covered her mouth with her sleeve and smiled faintly, speaking to Ji Yuan,

“I suddenly thought of something Uncle Ji might want! “

“Oh? I don’t even know myself, and you think you do?”

Ji Yuan glanced at her as he walked, also somewhat curious.

“Of course I know. Uncle Ji, you’re short a jar of immortal wine! “

Ji Yuan froze for a moment, then clapped his hands in regret,

“You’re right! I, too, suddenly remembered. Your father still owes me a whole jar of Ambergris. I didn’t ask for it last time I visited, and he shamelessly feigned sleep in the Dragon Pool. He should be charged interest! “

Tens of thousands of miles away, at the Dragon Pool, a massive dragon suddenly felt an unbearable itch in his nose.

“Achoo! Achoo! Achoo-ong! “

Rumble, rumble, rumble…

Outside the Dragon Pool, numerous aquatic creatures heard the violent dragon roar, and even the Yakshas guarding the Dragon Pool were suddenly swept away by torrents bursting from the depths of the Dragon Pool.

Countless aquatic creatures were tossed about in disarray, one or two pavilions in the aquatic palace collapsed, and countless artifacts were overturned. The water currents surged and churned across dozens of miles of the entire Tongtian River basin, river waves rose and fell erratically, and faint dragon roars continued to reverberate.

T/n: note to self: stay far away from the Tongtian River during flu season

Previously translated as spirit object. Might continue to flip between the two depending on context.

Chapter 334: Drumbeats and Echoes

After a while, the waters of the Tongtian River finally stabilized. Many of the still-dizzy aquatic creatures scattered, buzzing with questions, some even personally heading to the River God’s Palace to inquire about what was happening.

The Yakshas of the Underwater Palace, somewhat flustered, hurriedly returned to their posts, anxiously questioning each other.

“What just happened?”

“It seemed like an anomaly from the Dragon Pool!”

“Huh? His Majesty the Dragon Lord isn’t hurt, is he?”

“It’s currently unclear. The Commander has already gone to the Dragon Pool to check!”

Some of the Yakshas and aquatic creatures were restless, swimming back and forth within the Underwater Palace. Others went to salvage furniture and items from the collapsed palace sections, propping up fallen items.

They now suspected that the Underwater Palace had been attacked, a situation they had not encountered in hundreds of years. Casualties were still unknown, but it was clear that the Tongtian River’s turmoil had been widespread.

A long while later, a red-haired Yaksha wielding a long halberd emerged from entrance of the Dragon Pool’s. He looked at the concerned aquatic creatures.

“Everyone, disperse, disperse! It’s nothing serious. The Dragon Lord merely sneezed twice. The Dragon Lord asks us to restore the Underwater Palace to its original state and return to our respective posts.”

“Oh. . . so that’s it!”

“So it was the Dragon Lord sneezing…”

“As expected of the Dragon Lord! Just a few sneezes caused such a commotion in the Tongtian River. What would happen if he truly got angry?”

“Heh, I certainly don’t want to see the Dragon Lord angry.”

“Me neither!”

The Yakshas of the River God’s Underwater Palace and all the underwater beings now understood what had happened. They dispatched messengers to reassure the other aquatic creatures, and the small disturbance in the Tongtian River ultimately subsided.

However, the Old Dragon found himself unable to sleep in his own Dragon Pool, no matter how he tossed and turned.

The brilliant light of luminous pearls shone around him, and rich spiritual energy gathered from all directions, dense water essence spontaneously entwining itself around his dragon body. A pair of colossal, amber dragon eyes blinked once.

“Strange. Why can’t I stop sneezing? Am I under some kind of spell? No, that can’t be right. Could it be some cultivator with an extremely strong sympathetic connection, speaking ill of me behind my back? Who would possess such cultivation?”

The Old Dragon narrowed his eyes in thought, reviewing everything he had done recently, before suddenly blurting out:

“Could it be that fellow Ji Yuan?”

…

“Achoo…”

Aboard the Nine Peaks Mountain’s Realm-Crossing Flying Ferry, Ji Yuan had just settled down at a restaurant on the top deck market, when he couldn’t help but let out a mighty sneeze.

This left Chi Gui, Lin Jian, and even the Dragon Daughter Ying Ruoli around him wide-eyed in astonishment. The higher a cultivator’s cultivation level, the stronger their control over their bodily sensations. For an Immortal Cultivator of Ji Yuan’s caliber, sneezing was virtually impossible unless it was intentional!

“Uncle Ji, is something wrong with this restaurant?”

“Yes, Immortal Elder Ji, if you don’t like it, we’ll go to another one immediately!”

“Right, right, I know a restaurant at the far end of the market that often uses fruits and vegetables imbued with spiritual energy in their dishes!”

Seeing their expressions, Ji Yuan shifted in his seat, one hand rubbing his nose, the other raised and waving dismissively.

“It’s nothing, nothing serious. Just a sneeze. Someone must be praising me. Let’s eat, let’s eat!”

As he spoke, Ji Yuan reached out to beckon the shopkeeper over, who had been carefully serving them. The shopkeeper had personally come over from the moment they entered the establishment, but hadn’t dared to speak, fearing to disturb them.

Other mortals might not recognize Chi Gui and Lin Jian, but for someone whose family had run a tavern on the realm-crossing ferry for generations, not recognizing the two Masters of Nine Peaks Mountain would have shown a severe lack of discernment.

Seeing Chi Gui and Lin Jian personally bring guests to the establishment, he could basically understand that this man and woman were very likely the “whale riders of the sea” that everyone had just been talking about so excitedly.

Seeing Ji Yuan’s gesture, the shopkeeper naturally took a step closer.

“Esteemed guest, how may I serve you?”

Ji Yuan inquired:

“Your restaurant is located on a flying ship frequented by immortal cultivators; you must have some special skills. What are your signature dishes?”

“Er, haha, what are you saying, esteemed guest? We mostly serve common home-style dishes here, but we meticulously prepare them with care. As Master Lin just mentioned, we do indeed have dishes made with vegetables and fruits imbued with spiritual energy.”

Ji Yuan nodded, patting the table to indicate that the Dragon Daughter and the two Masters from Nine Peaks Mountain should sit as well.

“In that case, shopkeeper, just select a few of your favorite dishes and send them over. Around nine dishes with one soup should suffice. Oh, and two jugs of fine wine, please.”

“Right away!”

The shopkeeper replied, then stepped away to personally give instructions to the kitchen. After a moment, he returned once more to attend by their side.

Ji Yuan looked at him and couldn’t help but ask.

“Have you always lived here?”

The shopkeeper smiled.

“Some may have just arrived, others come and go, but my Ma family has been here for generations.”

As if knowing what Ji Yuan was about to ask, the shopkeeper continued.

“On this Immortal Ship, there are no harsh taxes or levies, no natural disasters or man-made calamities, and one can behold magnificent scenery rarely seen by ordinary mortals. Moreover, we live and thrive here, and if one day we can forge a good connection with an immortal elder, leading our descendants to embark on the path of immortality, then even our chickens and dogs might ascend to become celestial beings.”

Ji Yuan looked at Chi Gui.

“Are there such cases?”

The latter nodded.

“Indeed, there are. After all, the spiritual energy on the flying ship is somewhat more abundant than outside, so long-term residence here truly offers benefits. However, it’s actually best to land and live on the ground for a while every now and again before returning, as a flying ship moves through the heavens, unable to touch the ground, which ultimately presents a deficiency in terms of cultivating the Five Elements.”

The shopkeeper, who had been listening from the side, quickly bowed with cupped hands towards Ji Yuan and the others to express his gratitude.

He had heard such information before, but it was never truly confirmed. Now, hearing Chi Gui’s words, he knew it must be true.

Before long, several dishes, each with their own distinct color, aroma, and flavor, were brought out. The young waiter brought the trays to the table, and the shopkeeper personally served the dishes, with the two pots of fine wine arriving at the same time.

The two Masters from Nine Peaks Mountain merely symbolically touched their chopsticks; essentially, Ji Yuan was the only one eating. Ying Ruoli, meanwhile, stood by, sleeves pulled back, pouring him wine.

In the restaurant, Ji Yuan felt no embarrassment at all, simply eating heartily and drinking freely. Chi Gui and Lin Jian occasionally chatted and answered questions, while the Dragon Daughter ensured Ji Yuan’s wine glass was never empty. A few young waiters, dazed, stared blankly at the vibrant beauty of the Dragon Daughter.

This scene was quite harmonious and pleasant. If any painting connoisseurs were to have been present, they certainly would not have wanted to miss this sight.

It was just as the third round of wine was poured and the dishes had been half-eaten, a sudden loud whistle erupted from beneath the flying ship.

“Lady Ruoli—Mr. Ji—where have you gone? Did this strange flying ship abduct you? Hand over Mr. Ji and Lady Ruoli quickly… Rooo-woo… Rooo-woo-ooooooh…”1

A whale cry, both violent and strangely melodious, pierced the air, grating fiercely on the ears. Even aboard the flying ship, many mortals and cultivators covered their ears.

The dishes on Ji Yuan’s table rattled and shook.

“Clatter-clatter-clatter-clatter-clatter-clatter…”

Chi Gui formed hand seals, casting a technique in the air. In an instant, all sound vanished, likely cut off by the flying ship’s enchantments.

But the very next moment.

“Boom!”

With this sound, the dishes on the table lifted two inches directly into the air. The entire flying ship rocked violently, and some of the ordinary shopkeepers and patrons inside the restaurant staggered, some even falling to the ground.

“This fool is quite loyal, he’s gone mad. Ruoli, go calm him down.”

“Understood!”

The Dragon Daughter stood up, a slight amused smile on her face, and flew directly out after responding. As she was leaving, Chi Gui reopened the sound barrier, and the piercing, intense whale cry once again reverberated:

“Roooo-wooo-wooo…”

“General Giant Whale, stop making a fuss! Uncle Ji and I are guests on this flying ship!”

Though Ruoli’s clear, cool voice wasn’t loud, Ji Yuan could hear it distinctly. Sure enough, the whale cry ceased abruptly, replaced by a mixture of joyful and panicked calls.

This farce came and went quickly. Soon, the Dragon Daughter returned, while General Giant Whale continued to circle and swim beneath the flying ship, drawing many onlookers who peered over the ship’s rails.

In the dark night, visibility was quite good when aided by the flying ship’s glow and the starlight.

But this night was destined to be anything but peaceful. As Ji Yuan was still enjoying his rare meal, a sound like a drumbeat began to grow slowly louder, as if something was approaching.

“Boom… Boom… Boom… Boom…” . . .

It was a vast and echoing drumbeat, particularly noticeable in the darkness of the night and ocean, slowly approaching from afar. Ji Yuan’s chopsticks paused.

Ji Yuan looked around and found that Chi Gui and Lin Jian had no reaction. Even the Dragon Daughter who had just returned seemed completely unfazed. Seeing Ji Yuan looking at her with a strange expression, Ying Ruoli even asked with concern:

“Uncle Ji, what’s wrong?”

Ji Yuan furrowed his brow, put down his chopsticks, and looked at the two Nine Peaks Mountain Masters, then back to Ying Ruoli.

“Ruoli, and you two as well, can you hear a drumbeat?”

Ying Ruoli glanced at the two masters; their expressions showed they shared her thoughts. She then shook her head, saying,

The drumbeat grew louder and louder. Ji Yuan could no longer ignore it. He stood up directly and walked outside, continuing until he reached the ship’s rail, peering in the direction of the sound.

The night sky was pitch black, with stars twinkling brilliantly. Ji Yuan opened his supernatural eyes, channeling all his spiritual energy into his vision. On the horizon in his sight, a peculiar red color began to emerge.

Faintly visible, yet seemingly infinitely distant, it drifted like flickering flames.

There were no clouds, no discernible unusual phenomena. This patch of light drew closer from afar, and the drumbeat growing increasingly resonant.

“Boom… Boom… Boom…” . . .

“Do you see it? Do you hear it?”

Ying Ruoli strained her spiritual energy, looking intently in the direction of Ji Yuan’s gaze, but still found nothing. The two masters beside her were in the same predicament.

Ji Yuan’s gaze shifted. The red light on the horizon passed overhead, continuing eastward. It felt incredibly distant, and though it appeared slow, it was actually moving extremely fast.

Ji Yuan rose up onto a cloud, heading straight for the red light on the horizon. But no matter how he flew, the sense of distance didn’t diminish in the slightest.

The distant red light receded further and further, yet grew brighter and brighter. Just as the drumbeat was about to vanish…

“Ro-woo…”

General Giant Whale, beneath the flying ship, let out a faint whale call.

“Ohhhahh—”

On top of the drumbeat, a strange cry echoed from the distant red light. Then, the light vanished, and all sounds faded into nothingness.

‘What exactly was that on the first day of the New Year? ’

Ji Yuan looked down at the area beneath the flying ship. At this moment, General Giant Whale appeared unusually quiet.

‘Were those two calls an echo?’

T/n: more strange phenomena, wheee! JY is enjoying his foodie road trip of the cultivation world and would prefer not to be bothered by all the strange Main Character events preventing him from eating good food

Whale noises, LOL

Chapter 335: You’re Henpecked!

Ji Yuan immediately leaped off the side of the ship, landing lightly on General Giant Whale’s back. Ying Ruoli swiftly followed suit, while Chi Gui and Lin Jian hesitated for a moment before ultimately deciding not to descend with them.

Ji Yuan stood on the giant whale’s back and asked.

“General Giant Whale, did you hear the drumbeats just now, and see the red light in the sky? “

General Giant Whale’s current state was somewhat peculiar, and he hestiated before answering.

“To answer Mr. Ji, I did not hear any drumbeats, nor did I see any red light. But… but just now I felt extremely suppressed, almost unable to control myself, nearly dove into the deep sea. In the end, I involuntarily let out a cry, and then…”

The General Giant Whale strained his eyes upwards, looking at Ji Yuan on his back.

“Then I heard a strange cry. That sound, it scared this General senseless! Thankfully, it disappeared immediately! “

“You heard that cry? “

Ji Yuan was slightly surprised, but found that it made perfect sense.

“Indeed, I heard that strange cry. The sound was extremely eerie and a bit hoarse, as if it was close at hand yet also far away. I wasn’t even this scared when the Dragon Mother was angry!”

The surface of the ocean beneath the giant whale rippled for a moment. He asked Ji Yuan with lingering fear.

“Mr. Ji, you are a highly accomplished Taoist, widely experienced and knowledgeable. Do you know what that sound was? What kind of demon was it? “

“Wait, you heard it too? Why didn’t I hear it? “

Ying Ruoli questioned General Giant Whale incredulously. The General Giant Whale’s cultivation was insignificant compared to hers, so how could this simple-minded whale hear it?

“Ah? Lady Ruoli couldn’t hear it? “

General Giant Whale was even more surprised. He quickly explained the feeling of oppressive terror he had just experienced to Ying Ruoli, who then looked thoughtfully at Ji Yuan.

This matter was highly unusual; even Uncle Ji seemed a bit baffled by it.

“There’s no need to worry too much about this anymore. Since we have no further clues, let’s not dwell on it. I haven’t paid for the meal yet; I’ll go back and settle the bill. “

Back on the flying ship, Ji Yuan returned to the restaurant directly. After paying, he had no further interest in lingering, and prepared to leave immediately.

Actually, Ji Yuan had originally wanted to experience riding Nine Peaks Mountain’s realm-crossing flying ship, but this trip had a clear purpose, and their destinations were different. Moreover, he couldn’t possibly abandon General Giant Whale and travel by ship himself. This was a flying ship, it rarely sailed on the sea, and it was impossible for General Giant Whale to follow along.

The prices for food on the immortal flying ship were indeed exorbitant, costing a full ten taels of silver. Ji Yuan’s savings were almost instantly cut down by more than half. However, the ordinary dishes and wine accounted for less than one tael; the real bulk of the cost was that plate of so-called “Immortal Qi-Infused Seasonal Vegetables.”

He couldn’t discern any special taste, yet the price was ridiculously high.

As they meant to establish a good relationship, the two Nine Peaks Mountain Masters naturally escorted him to the side of the ship.

“Mr. Ji, it is destined fate that we met. I observe that your immortal aura is pure and harmonious, truly that of a highly virtuous, powerful cultivator. Our Nine Peaks Mountain is located in Heng Province, in the northern border region. Every sixty years, we hold a Cultivator’s Grand Assembly. Judging from the current date, there aren’t many years left before then; it will begin in the summer of the year of Wuxu in the calendar of Dazhen. If Senior has some free time then, you are welcome to visit our Nine Peaks Mountain. “

As Chi Gui spoke, he took out a bamboo slip from his person and handed it to Ji Yuan with both hands.

“Mr. Ji, this is a command token from our Nine Peaks Mountain. If you come to visit, the mountain gate will guide you to either me or Lin Jian. We hope Senior will grace us with your presence when you have time; you are welcome at any time, not just during the Cultivator’s Grand Assembly! “

Ji Yuan did not refuse. He took it, inspecting it, and saw that the bamboo slip bore the four characters “Nine Peaks Token of Decree,” imbued with a special barrier.

“Alright, I will certainly visit when I have time! “

Ji Yuan bowed with cupped hands as he took the bamboo slip.

“Then we shall take our leave. No need to see us off! “

“Mr. Ji, take care! “

The two masters saw them off. Ji Yuan nodded once again before flying away from the realm-crossing ferry. Ying Ruoli, on the other hand, didn’t even bother to say goodbye to the two and simply flew down.

The bamboo tokens and warm invitations the two masters from Nine Peaks Mountain had extended were only to Ji Yuan, clearly not including the Dragon Daughter in their invitation list. Although the Dragon Daughter didn’t care for it, a hint of displeasure still lingered in her heart, so much so that she didn’t even bother to feign politeness.

As they stepped once more onto the solid back of General Giant Whale, the realm-crossing ferry had already flown to the side. On the flying ship, Chi Gui, Lin Jian, and some other cultivators from Nine Peaks Mountain bowed respectfully in Ji Yuan’s direction. Ji Yuan merely nodded, not returning the gesture.

“Hmph, most cultivators are like this, holding many prejudices against us monsters.”

Seeing Ying Ruoli’s rare display of petulance, Ji Yuan smiled and consoled her.

“In the end, inherent prejudices are like this. If you had revealed your identity as the River Goddess, perhaps you would have been treated better. Besides, if they had truly invited you just now, and you had actually gone, it might have only led to awkwardness. Better not to be invited at all.”

“I don’t care, anyway! General Giant Whale, what are you gaping at? Hurry up and swim!”

“Oh, oh, oh! Yes, ma’am! “

Hearing Ying Ruoli’s angry words, General Giant Whale dared not delay. He quickly flicked his massive tail.

“Boom!” A huge wave crashed behind him, and the giant whale’s body shot forward. By now, the realm-crossing ferry had flown further and further away, leaving only a tiny, bright silhouette of a ship in the night.

Ji Yuan had lost all interest in writing. Instead, he stood on the whale’s back, letting the sea breeze buffet him, his thoughts drifting to the horizon.

What exactly was that strange drumbeat, and what was that cry? Why could General Giant Whale hear it, but why couldn’t he hear it completely? What lay in that direction in the Eastern Sea?

Ji Yuan believed that such a strange occurrence at the transition between the old and new year must have a reason behind it, and that his ability to witness it might somehow be related to General Giant Whale’s intensely loud whale cry from earlier.

“Alas. . . human power has its limits; it’s difficult to fully comprehend all the mysteries of the world!”

Ji Yuan sighed. The Dragon Daughter looked at him, wanting to say something, but ultimately remained silent.

…

In the Tongtian River’s Dragon Palace, Ying Feng, the Dragon Son who had gone to visit and drink with some of his friends, returned to the underwater palace.

As soon as his dragon form swam into the forbidden area of the underwater palace, he found that several wings had actually collapsed, and some areas appeared to have been newly built.

The flood dragon transformed into a man in brocade robes, grabbing a passing half-fish girl.

“What happened? Did someone dare to attack my Tongtian River Dragon Palace? “

“Your Highness, you have returned? It was not someone that someone dared to attack the underwater palace, but rather that the Dragon Lord sneezed twice in his sleep, which completely destroyed this section of the Tongtian River. “

Ying Feng nodded knowingly.

“Oh, I see. Father can actually sneeze? I’ve never seen him sneeze all these years. What a pity…”

Ying Feng asked no further questions and went straight to the main hall. He flopped down onto a large, comfortable chair, propped his feet on the armrest, reached for a handful of pastries, and munched on them. Then he pulled out a flask of wine from his robes. After sniffing it, he carefully opened his mouth and drank a tiny sip from the water.

“Tsk tsk tsk aah… I can’t drink too much, I can’t drink too much, this is for Uncle Ji! “

This translucent white jade flask was a “thousand-dou pot,” which was considered a treasure. As the name implied, despite its small size, it could hold a thousand “dou” of wine, which was quite a lot.1

“Hehe, Uncle Ji will definitely like it! If he’s in a good mood, he might teach me the lightning technique, then I’ll…”

Ying Feng happily thought to himself again. Through his drinking buddy Gao Tianming, he had already learned of several other great divine abilities his Uncle Ji had never revealed. Among them, the True Fire of Samadhi was naturally terrifying, but what interested Ying Feng most was lightning techniques.

He wandered around the palace a few times, but couldn’t find his sister.

“Where’s Ruoli? “

Ying Feng frowned. His sister rarely went out, except when helping his father summon clouds and rain. Using language from Ji Yuan’s previous life, she was a complete homebody. But today, he had been back for so long, and he hadn’t seen his sister come out yet.

“Hey, you, have you seen Ruoli? “

Ying Feng asked a fish-girl who was bringing over new pastries. She shook her head repeatedly.

“The River Goddess has not been in the underwater palace recently. This servant does not know where she went! “

Ying Feng frowned, rose from his seat, and went outside, where he saw a Yaksha approaching. He asked them directly.

“Do you know where Ruoli went? “

The two Yakshas, who had come to report to him, naturally stopped and bowed in reply.

“Replying to His Highness, some time ago, Mr. Ji visited and invited the Dragon Lord, the River Goddess, and His Highness to a certain place. The River Goddess went along at that time. “

“Ah? My father went too? I’m the only one who didn’t go? “

The Yaksha replied hurriedly but cautiously.

“The Dragon Lord was deep in sleep. Mr. Ji and the River Goddess called several times, but he did not awaken, so he is still in the Dragon Pool. “

Ying Feng’s eyes widened as he looked towards the Dragon Pool behind the palace.

“My father didn’t wake up? Nonsense! He must have been feigning sleep! Uncle Ji came looking for him, and he didn’t go? There’s definitely something strange! “

Even in the water, the two Yakshas almost broke into a cold sweat, quickly lowering their heads and fixing their gaze downwards.

Ying Feng pressed on for more information.

“Then where did they go? I’ll chase after them! “

“I heard… I heard they went to the very edge of the Eastern Sea, near the Northern Sea, where a whale demon named General Giant Whale sought help from Mr. Ji, hoping the underwater palace here could rescue the Dragon Mother. “

“My mother—! “

Ying Feng cried out, a hint of panic flashing across his face.

“What happened? What happened to my mother? Why did they seek help from Uncle Ji, why didn’t they come directly to the Tongtian River? Forget it, just tell me how to get there, I’ll go find them! “

“Your Highness, don’t worry! Your Highness, don’t worry! Mr. Ji and the River Goddess have been gone for a long time. We do not know their route, and you wouldn’t be able to catch up with their speed, nor would you know which direction to go! “

Another Yaksha further advised.

“Yes, Your Highness, you don’t need to worry. With Mr. Ji accompanying them, it’s no different from the Dragon Lord going personally. Nothing will happen! “

Ying Feng paced back and forth, clapping his hands together forcefully.

“Ugh, I’ll go find my father! “

Ying Feng, with hurried steps, arrived directly outside the Dragon Pool. Seeing his enraged expression, the Yakshas nearby dared not stop him, but as Ying Feng tried to rush in, he was bounced back by a water bubble.

“Dad! Stop pretending to be asleep! Let me in! If not, at least tell me how to get to Mother’s side! Dad—Dad, you’re going too far! “

Ying Feng angrily punched the Dragon Pool’s entrance, only making a dent in the bubble barrier, which quickly restored itself.

“Dad, you… you, you, you… you’re henpecked!”

“ROAR! You scoundrel—”

The old dragon’s furious roar echoed.

Rumble…

Large waves surged out, directly sweeping Ying Feng away. This section of the Tongtian River once again shook, and the entire underwater palace suffered another disaster…

T/n: RIP the underwater construction budget. At least they have a lot of pearls to make up for it. Also RIP Ying Feng, poor guy just can’t seem to get lucky with JY…

1 dou = 10 liters, 1000 dou = 10 000 L/2642 gallons, about the size of a small swimming pool.

Chapter 336: This Again!

The turbulent underwater currents swept Ying Feng and the yakshas away, causing the back half of the underwater palace to sway precariously.

Fortunately, the Old Dragon had some control this time, preventing the palace from collapsing. Still, it thoroughly terrified the aquatic denizens of the underwater palace, especially with the True Dragon’s aura, which made countless river creatures tremble.

Ying Feng was directly swept out of the underwater palace by the torrent, along with several other yakshas and aquatic creatures who were similarly disoriented.

The dragon prince shook his head, looking somewhat fearfully towards the Dragon Pool at the back of the palace. He didn’t know what had possessed him just now to dare use such a provocation on his father.

The underwater currents quickly subsided. A yaksha cautiously addressed Ying Feng.

“Your Highness, the Dragon Lord…”

“Just pretend you heard nothing just now, and don’t go spreading it around, or my father will devour you! “

Ying Feng composed himself and replied.

“Yes, yes, yes!”

“This subordinate heard nothing!”

After calming himself down in the water, Ying Feng swam back to the underwater palace. Before he had even reached the dragon pool, he saw his old man emerge from within, his face still flushed with anger.

Ying Feng quickly approached and bowed, his demeanor utterly respectful, as if he had forgotten what he had just said.

“Father! “

The Old Dragon squinted and glanced at him, grunting a nasal “hmph,” before walking towards the main hall. Ying Feng naturally hastened to follow.

As they reached the main hall, fish-maids were hurriedly tidying up items that had been shaken onto the floor, righting overturned chairs and tables.

The Old Dragon paid it no mind, settling onto his precious couch.

“What was it you just said? Your father was half-asleep and didn’t quite catch it. Say it again for me to hear?”

Ying Feng’s heart pounded wildly. That had been an impulsive outburst; how would he dare repeat it now?

“I just wanted to know where Ruoli and Uncle Ji went. I wanted to ask Father if you could tell me their location. Since it’s inconvenient for you to go, I could follow and take a look.”

As he spoke, Ying Feng picked up a cup and teapot from a fish servant’s tray nearby and poured a cup of hot tea for his father.

The Old Dragon snorted coldly as he took the teacup, observing the bubble around the cup that prevented the water from mixing. He neither drank the tea nor spoke, lost in thought for a long time.

“Your sister’s cultivation is higher than yours, and she has Ji Yuan accompanying her, so there should be no problem. Even if you were to pursue them now, you might not be able to find them.”

“But, this…”

Ying Feng paced back and forth.

“Father, aren’t you worried at all? I don’t believe you lack contingency plans. If you know their location or something, at least point me in a direction! “

The Old Dragon raised the teacup and took a small sip.

“No. Just wait for news. If one Ji Yuan isn’t enough, then what use would a hundred or so of you be?”

Although the Old Dragon spoke these words, his Mirage Concealment Technique followed General Giant Whale from the distance like a shadow, disappearing into the water on clear days and transforming into mist when clouds gathered and the wind picked up.

Of course, given the old dragon’s apprehension of Ji Yuan’s keen perception, he maintained quite a far distance between them; he could only barely avoid losing track of them.

…

Although navigational instruments like the compass1 had long existed, few ships ventured into the open ocean. Aside from encountering the Nine Peaks Mountain Realm-Crossing Ferry a few days ago, Ji Yuan and the Dragon Daughter had not come across any further “man-made traces” for over a month now.

The General Giant Whale’s speed was remarkably swift. Despite his enormous size, his underwater movement techniques were unique; he could smoothly navigate peculiar currents in various waters, and the disorienting mist that rose in some areas did not affect his sense of direction in the slightest.

In the vast ocean, there were naturally some aquatic monsters, but because the sea was simply too vast, even if there were a large number of them, they would appear extremely sparse when spread throughout.

However, the direction in which the General Giant Whale brought Ji Yuan and Ying Ruoli occasionally passed through the territories of some aquatic spirits. Some spirits would come out to observe, while others ignored them, but there was generally no conflict, not even verbal interaction.

In Ji Yuan’s opinion, many of these sea monsters seemed more laid-back than their terrestrial counterparts, or perhaps it was simply because they had too few opportunities to encounter humans.

It was early morning, and a dense fog had risen over the sea. This naturally posed no problem for General Giant Whale, whose direction remained steadfastly unchanged.

The Dragon Daughter sat cross-legged on a meditation cushion on the whale’s back, cultivating with her eyes closed. Ji Yuan, meanwhile, held a book in one hand and a brush in the other. He wasn’t writing at a desk; instead, he sat cross-legged, resting the book on his knees, and continued to deduce his profound techniques.

The dense fog also had a negligible effect on Ji Yuan; he couldn’t see very clearly anyway under normal circumstances. In fact, his vision in the fog might even significantly surpass that of ordinary people.

All around him was the sound of the waves. Ji Yuan was immersed in his deductions of the Tao. It was precisely at this moment…

“Boom… boom… boom… boom…”

A series of drumbeats drifted from afar.

Ji Yuan had been a bit sensitive to the sound of drumbeats lately. The moment he heard the sound, he almost immediately stood up.

“Uncle Ji, what’s wrong?”

Ying Ruoli opened her eyes and stood up to ask Ji Yuan.

“There are drumbeats!”

“Drumbeats? Could it be like last time again? “

Ji Yuan furrowed his brow and listened carefully for a moment, then spoke with some uncertainty.

“It’s more than one drum… General Giant Whale, head in this direction. “

As he spoke, Ji Yuan pointed in the direction from which the sound was coming. Beneath him, General Giant Whale responded with a grunt and changed course, swimming in that direction.

Not long after, the drumbeats grew clearer. By now, even Ying Ruoli could hear them.

“There really are drumbeats! “

Ying Ruoli’s confirmation made Ji Yuan realize that these drumbeats were not the same as those he had heard on the first day of the Lunar New Year. It seemed he had been overthinking things.

Approximately a few miles from Ji Yuan and his companions, a massive fleet of ships was sailing.

Some of the larger ships were nearly forty to fifty zhang long, surrounded by no fewer than a hundred vessels of various sizes. The drumbeats were coming from these very ships.

On the largest ship in the center, two massive drums were being vigorously beaten by drummers wielding drumsticks.

“Boom… boom… boom… boom…”

“The drumming must not stop! The drumming must not stop! The fleet will use the drums to find the formation in the fog! Drummers, take turns to rest and rotate every five hundred strikes! The drumming must not stop! “

A man in a large cloak shouted commands from a high position at the stern of the ship.

It seemed drummers were beating drums on all the surrounding ships, using the sound to guide the entire fleet, thus preventing any single vessel from getting lost. They were also using the varying intensity of the drumbeats to maintain a subtle sense of distance, preventing collisions.

From General Giant Whale’s current position, the drumbeats were already quite loud for the three of them. Though the dense fog obstructed their view, it had little effect on Ji Yuan and his companions.

“What a massive fleet! “

Ying Ruoli couldn’t help but exclaim. This was the first time she had seen so many ships sailing together since they had ventured out to sea.

“Indeed. This is a massive mortal fleet. To think they’ve sailed all the way into the depths of the Eastern Sea! Where could they be headed? “

“Mister Ji, I can approach a little closer for a look! “

Seeing that both the Dragon Daughter and Ji Yuan were interested, and also because he himself was somewhat curious, General Giant Whale suggested approaching. Seeing that no one objected, he quickly swam closer to the fleet.

The massive fleet was clearly moving quite slowly at the moment, with the sails of most of the ships furled.

For these ordinary people, the fog still had a significant impact. Even with many skilled martial arts experts, their vision would still be extremely limited. The Giant Whale General had already reached the vicinity of several medium-sized ships at the edge of the fleet, yet no one had noticed him.

On the nearest ship, besides the continuous drumming, someone was loudly reminding the crew.

“The Grand Commander said that the stranger the weather, the more cautious we must be of monsters appearing in the sea. Last month, three ships were lured away by monsters, and in the end, they were destroyed with all their crew lost. Everyone, be careful!”

The shouts from the ship naturally reached the ears of Ji Yuan and his companions.

Judging from the current situation, unless it was a demon that had gained significant power, no monster would provoke such a massive fleet. If what the people on the ship were shouting was true, then they must have encountered demons with considerable cultivation.

The General Giant Whale did not merely linger on the periphery. Instead, he carried Ji Yuan and Ying Ruoli, gliding around the entire fleet, pausing by everything, from the outer medium-sized and smaller ships to some of the larger vessels, allowing Ji Yuan to overhear many conversations on each of these ships.

Among them were expressions of longing for home, confusion about the future, and irritable temperaments. Few held firm beliefs.

This was an ocean-faring fleet from a place called Daxiu. Ji Yuan wasn’t sure if it was a dynasty from the Eastern Cloud Continent, but he suspected it was another continent entirely.

This fleet had passed through several countries and visited numerous island nations on their journey. They had been drifting at sea for eight full years, navigating many dangerous waters, and encountered countless predicaments. The fleet’s size had also dwindled from over two hundred ships to fewer than one hundred today.

Such a voyage could be described as fraught with peril and untold hardships, yet the fleet’s mission remained unfulfilled.

At this very moment, the Grand Commander of the fleet stood at the bow of the central ship, hands clasped behind his back, gazing forward, his eyes filled with confusion as he muttered to himself.

“Where exactly is the Xianxia Island that the Imperial Preceptor spoke of? Can it truly harbor an elixir of immortality? It has been eight years. Even if we find it, how long would it take to return? Is His Majesty still alive? Can we… can we even make it back? “

Unbeknownst to the Grand Commander, beneath the bow of his ship floated an enormous whale, almost half the size of his ship, carrying two people on its back.

Ji Yuan sighed and shook his head.

“This again.”

T/n: LOL JY is sick and tired of emperors looking for immortality

Mango rabbit-hole time! Ancient China had their own versions of magnetic compasses, with the earliest recorded example being a magnetic needle floating in a bowl of water, dated to the Han Dynasty (206 BC – 220 AD). Astrolabes and the like were European inventions, while the Chinese tended towards armillary spheres, boards and bamboo rulers to orient by the stars (similar in theory to the astrolabe, I believe), and dead reckoning (estimating position based on course and speed). They also had significant documentation on star charts, navigation, and current movements.

Chapter 337: Time to Go Home

Ying Ruoli had heard about some previous events, so she naturally understood why Ji Yuan was sighing.

“Since they know of Xianxia Island, it seems this is no aimless, haphazard search.”

Ji Yuan only half-agreed with the Dragon Daughter’s statement. He replied:

“For the Imperial Preceptor of the Dynasty of Daxiu to mobilize such a massive fleet, he must be quite trusted. But whether this search will lead to Xianxia Island or not is another matter. They also might not necessarily obtain any divine elixirs or miraculous elixirs.”

Ying Ruoli smiled.

“Then, Uncle Ji, you may be unaware of something.”

“Oh? What makes you say that?”

Ji Yuan inquired, slightly surprised. He watched as the Dragon Daughter turned to look at the Grand Commander at the bow of the large ship.

“Even I, a flood dragon who perpetually resides in the Tongtian River, have heard of Xianxia Island. My elder brother once had a drinking buddy who was suppressed by an immortal cultivator from Xianxia Island. Rumor has it that this island is located in the Eastern Sea; there are also whispers that it’s in the Northern Sea, and some even say it might be in the Outer Wild Ocean…”

Ji Yuan frowned. The rumors he had heard before stated that Xianxia Island was in the Eastern Sea.

“Could it be that Xianxia Island can move?”

“I am not certain, but I have indeed heard various such claims. I have also heard stories of mortals accidentally landing on the island and receiving gifts from immortals. These people all safely returned to their hometowns, and some of these accounts are verifiable; not everything was fabricated.”

The Dragon Daughter paused here before continuing.

“In contrast, ordinary cultivators, without any connection to the island, even if they possess the ability to fly, might not necessarily find Xianxia Island if they rashly go searching for it.”

Ji Yuan looked thoughtfully towards the bow of the ship.

“So the Imperial Preceptor of the Dynasty of Daxiu actually has some tricks up his sleeve. Having them sail the seas in such a desperate search is partly to try his luck at stumbling across Xianxia Island, and also partly to move the immortal cultivators of Xianxia Island with their sincerity.”

From the Dragon Daughter’s brief remarks just now, Ji Yuan could basically confirm that the immortal cultivators of Xianxia Island were for the most part quite benevolent. He had also heard rumors of Xianxia Island cultivators slaying demons.

Ying Ruoli looked at the long fleet of ships, stretching before and behind them, shrouded in mist.

“As Uncle Ji said, it’s still just a gamble. Perhaps for the Dynasty of Daxiu, staking their slim hope on sending such a fleet is a worthwhile venture.”

Ji Yuan nodded.

“Indeed. Or perhaps this isn’t the only thing the Dynasty of Daxiu is betting on. Who knows, there might be many capable individuals in the imperial court, such as that Imperial Preceptor who understands Xianxia Island’s customs.”

Ying Ruoli sighed in a rare display of emotion.

“That makes sense. It’s just that these people are too pitiful…”

Ji Yuan looked at the Dragon Daughter. She was a flood dragon serving as a River Goddess, in the end; had it been any other monster, these people’s lives or deaths wouldn’t matter to them in the slightest.

“I have witnessed imperial families before. While I cannot make sweeping generalizations, I must say that for the emperor of a great dynasty, the lives of a whole fleet of people should be of little concern.”

Even if there were fewer than a hundred ships left, the people on board still numbered in the tens of thousands. Who knows, perhaps that same number of people had already perished.

“Judging from this person’s demeanor as he spoke, there was no sign of enthusiasm or determination. He must have lost all hope in this mission. How about this we try to persuade them?”

“Persuade? How? We don’t have any elixirs of immortality.”

The Dragon Daughter looked at Ji Yuan, puzzled.

“Does Xianxia Island have any? They’ve searched bitterly for eight years; now they are merely held here by imperial decree, unable to leave. They would all want to return home if given any chance.”

“They persevered for eight years without turning back. Do you think they would listen?”

The Dragon Daughter looked at Ji Yuan. Was her Uncle Ji planning to use his supernatural abilities to threaten them, or even directly use his techniques to force them to return?

Ji Yuan merely smiled. Ying Ruoli, in the end, still had little experience of the outside world.

“Rather than saying they’re searching for immortal elixirs, it’s better to say they’re searching for an immortal island. And rather than searching for an immortal island, ultimately, they are searching for an immortal. Once they meet an immortal, what difference does it make whether it’s in the sea or on an island? The words of this ‘immortal’ should carry some weight, right?”

Hearing this, Ying Ruoli suddenly understood. She had never considered herself an “immortal,” and just now she had been blind to the fact that she hadn’t thought of her close Uncle Ji an immortal. But thinking it over carefully, whether Xianxia Island even had immortals of Uncle Ji’s caliber and attainment was debatable anyway.

The fleet continued to move slowly, and the sound of drums never stopped. But after a while, the fog began to gradually dissipate.

As soon as the fog dispersed, the ocean surface immediately became much clearer. The previously somewhat depressed crew members’ spirits also brightened, and the drummers with aching arms were finally able to rest.

The Grand Commander breathed a sigh of relief, but a voice suddenly rang out beside him.

“Sir, you must be the commander of this fleet, correct?”

“Who?!”

The Grand Commander was startled by the voice, immediately jumping back two steps. A group of armed guards immediately gathered around him.

However, the Grand Commander quickly raised his hand to stop his subordinates’ movements.

The newcomer wore a wide-sleeved green robe, his long hair flowing down his back, adorned with a jade hairpin. When he turned to look at the Grand Commander, the latter could see that his face was clean-shaven, his age indiscernible. The pair of pale eyes, widen open and staring directly at him, were particularly striking.

The Grand Commander hand touched the jade pendant hanging at his side. At such close proximity, he still didn’t feel any burning heat, indicating that the other party was not a demon.

His heart pounded as the Grand Commander carefully asked.

“Sir, when did you board the ship, and when did you arrive by my side?”

Ji Yuan replied bluntly.

“Roughly around the time when you were lamenting the endless wait for your return home.”

“Then, Sir, are you an immortal from Xianxia Island? Is the immortal island nearby?”

Everyone around around at this question, but the sea stretched endlessly into the distance, without even a single break in the horizon.

Ji Yuan glanced at the jade pendant on the Grand Commander’s waist. He hadn’t noticed it before, but the other man’s movement had caught his attention. Now, he could tell that it seemed to be something quite special.

“No need to look around. Xianxia Island is not nearby, nor am I an immortal from Xianxia Island.”

After a slight disappointment, the Grand Commander immediately perked back up.

“Then, Immortal Master, you are indeed an immortal, aren’t you?”

Ji Yuan smiled. This man was quite shrewd.

“By ordinary standards, I am indeed qualified to be an immortal.”

The Grand Commander immediately bowed deeply. Behind him, the guards and crew members followed suit, hastily bowing with cupped hands.

“I am Qiao Yong, the Imperial Grand Commander of Daxiu. Greetings, Immortal Master!”

“Greetings, Immortal Master!”

Ji Yuan didn’t flinch. He accepted the bow, gazing at Qiao Yong.

“That jade pendant on your waist is quite interesting. I heard that you encountered evil spirits and demons during your voyage. Aren’t you afraid that I might be a demon in disguise?”

Qiao Yong stood up and shook his head.

“This jade pendant was bestowed upon me by the Imperial Preceptor of Daxiu. It is called the Xiezhi1 Pendant. It can distinguish transformed demons and evil spirits, and can also differentiate between immortals and mortals. If a demon or evil spirit were to approach, the pendant would grow scorching hot. If an immortal were to arrive, the pendant would become cool and comfortable. For mortals, there is no reaction.”

“Oh!”

Ji Yuan nodded in understanding. This certainly saved him a lot of trouble.

However, Qiao Yong hadn’t explained the full story. Before he had set sail, the Imperial Preceptor gave him a bunch of talismans and artifacts, explained their uses to him in those few words, then added one more instruction at the end:

If they were to encounter someone who was clearly extraordinary at a glance, but the jade pendant still showed no reaction, then the visitor was naturally even more extraordinary than the pendant. Whether the visitor brought fortune or misfortune was left for him to judge.

However, Ji Yuan was intrigued by the name of this jade pendant.

“Xiezhi?”

If someone else where to hear this name, they might simply be wondering how to write it, but Ji Yuan immediately recognized it. Wasn’t this precisely a divine beast that could distinguish between good and evil, between right and wrong? However, the source of this knowledge was from his previous life.

“Immortal Master, the name is written like this…”

Qiao Yong merely gestured a couple of times, and Ji Yuan knew he hadn’t guessed wrong. So he waved his hand, stopping him.

“I know how to write it. This jade pendant was given by your Imperial Preceptor?”

“Precisely!”

“Where is the Dynasty of Daxiu located?”

“To reply to the Immortal Master, our Dynasty of Daxiu is located in the northern region of the Heng Continent.2 To the south is our great imperial court!”

Ji Yuan gleaned a lot from Qiao Yong’s few simple words. Qiao Yong could actually identify it as the ‘northern region of the Heng Continent,’ and their Imperial Advisor appeared to be quite skilled. Clearly, this dynasty was not simple. At the very least, these people were was far stronger than some people on the Eastern Cloud Continent, who believed their land to be the only one in the world.

At this moment, Ji Yuan had already decided: he would first inquire about the Xiezhi. If he couldn’t find out anything elsewhere, he would go ask that Imperial Preceptor. But for now, he would stick to his original plan.

“I heard you are searching for Xianxia Island, to seek the immortal elixir?”

“Yes! If we don’t succeed, then we will never be able to return home. The imperial decree is as heavy as a mountain. . .”

Ji Yuan nodded, and spoke calmly.

“Then you should return. Go back and tell your Imperial Preceptor that I will be attending a Cultivator’s Grand Assembly on Nine Peaks Mountain in a few years, and that the immortal cultivators of Xianxia Island will also attend. At that time, I will help you inquire if this mission can be accomplished. Since he knows of Xianxia Island, he should also be aware of the Cultivator’s Grand Assembly. Have your Imperial Preceptor convey this to the emperor.”

Since the imperial decree demanded that they achieve something, then Ji Yuan’s words actually counted as a gain — they could even be called a great achievement. As long as there were people like that Imperial Preceptor involved, they would surely pass the test. At worst, they would only suffer a minor reprimand, which was far better than drifting at sea for the rest of their lives and eventually dying in a foreign land.

Qiao Yong and his surrounding subordinates exchanged glances.

“Ah… May I ask for the esteemed Immortal Master’s name?”

“My surname is Ji. People call me Mr. Ji. Whether you believe this matter or not is up to you. That is all I have to say; I will take my leave now.”

With that, Ji Yuan leapt from the bow in a single stride. Clouds formed where he stepped, and he soared into the sky. A little further away, the ocean surface rippled, and a massive creature parted the waters and emerged. It was a giant whale, and on its back, there was another person, not clearly visible.

As the immortal in green robes descended, the person on the back of the sea beast bowed respectfully with cupped hands towards the man. Then, the two rode together on the whale’s back, departing in a different direction into the sea. In mere moments, they were out of sight.

The Xiezhi is a mythical creature that looks like an ox/goat with a long horn on its forehead. It has the power to judge good and evil, and spears corrupt officials on its horn.

恒 (héng): permanent/constant/fixed/ordinary/one of 64 hexagrams

Chapter 338: To Echo One Another

Because the fog had completely dispersed, visibility was excellent. Moreover, Ji Yuan had flown directly overhead, not too high above the fleet, and General Giant Whale emerging from the sea had caused a massive stir. The scene of the giant whale bearing the immortal away was witnessed by virtually all the crew members on the decks of every ship.

With Ji Yuan’s departure, a difficult decision fell upon Qiao Yong and the entire fleet. However, even before Qiao Yong could summon the key personnel from each ship, nearby vessels were already repeatedly signaling or using simple gestures to inquire.

Qiao Yong gazed into the distance, then looked glanced around.

“Do you want to go home? ”

The adjutants and crew members by his side all looked at him. Not one of them answered, yet the yearning in their eyes was palpable.

“Alright, gather the captains. Prepare to discuss our return. If there’s truly responsibility to bear, I will shoulder it! All of you, go! ”

“Your subordinates obey your command! ”

The adjutants beside him responded with excitement.

Originally, returning like this would have condemned the entire fleet, but now, with the help of that immortal surnamed Ji, it was highly likely they could be spared punishment, or at least limit the consequences.

Qiao Yong’s gaze swept towards the southwest. They had circled the seas for a long time overseas, but with the compass and constant charting, they knew the general direction to go in to head back. In this direction, to the southwest, should be the legendary Eastern Cloud Continent, though how far away it still was remained unknown.

…

On the back of the General Giant Whale, Ying Ruoli turned her head to look at the distant fleet.

“Uncle Ji, they just believed it like that? Mortals’ desires are very strong — even if it’s not their own desire, they fear the desire of an emperor.”

Of course, the Dragon Daughter wasn’t doubting Uncle Ji’s credibility. It was just that for something they had sought bitterly for eight years at immense cost, how could it be so easily dismissed by a single sentence, even if they had seen an immortal?

Ji Yuan didn’t look back, merely stating calmly.

“When I first left, they were about fifty percent convinced. After they gathered their important officials to discuss the matter, they would be about seventy percent convinced. When those officials return to their respective ships and the news spreads throughout the fleet, they will be a hundred percent convinced.

“As for what happens afterward, it’s actually not hard to predict. If the old emperor isn’t dead, given his desires, he wouldn’t do anything to them upon learning this news. If the old emperor has died, the new emperor will likely show leniency to these wandering subjects, especially with that Imperial Preceptor there. ”

Ying Ruoli merely thought for a moment, then smiled and stopped worrying. Instead, she tapped her foot on General Giant Whale.

“You’ve been swimming for almost two months already, how much longer? ”

“Lady Ruoli, don’t fret, don’t fret. We’ll be out of the Eastern Sea soon. However, once we pass the Desolate Sea, the ocean will become even wilder. The aquatic races there, without the restraints placed on them by the True Dragons of the Four Seas, will be equally wild. Please be cautious, Your Highness and Mr. Ji.”

Ying Ruoli acknowledged his words calmly.

“Do you think I have never been to the Desolate Sea? As for Uncle Ji, you needn’t worry about him at all. ”

“Yes, yes! ”

General Giant Whale swam with all his might, his speed increasing even more than before.

After another ten days or so, the color of the seawater began to subtly change, appearing a bit murkier. The waves, indeed, grew more tumultuous, with strong winds raging almost constantly. Sometimes, even a wild gale would sweep down a low altitude above the sea surface, causing monstrous waves to surge.

This was merely the state of the oceans on the surface. Although the seabed appeared much more stable, and fish and shrimp were abundant, the spiritual water energy also seemed to change, swirling through the turbulent currents like underwater winds. If one didn’t find a calm spot, it was not easy to cultivate with it.

General Giant Whale clearly struggled to swim here. Wild gusts of wind raged in places with towering waves, and whenever this happened, the General Giant Whale would dive to the depths, using his swimming ability to navigate underwater.

What had originally been a smooth journey now became extraordinarily bumpy. Any ordinary person on the whale’s back would have felt dizzy and nauseous.

There were still some calm places, but General Giant Whale said such places were likely favored by the demon races of the Desolate Sea, and it was best to avoid trouble and simply bypass them.

Ji Yuan and Ying Ruoli also agreed on this point, so, after traversing this small stretch of the Desolate Sea for a few days, they finally entered the relatively calmer Four Seas once more. This time, it was the Northern Sea.

The Eastern Sea and Northern Sea actually only shared a small stretch of border. A significant portion of their borders were separated by the Desolate Sea, though they weren’t too far apart. Now that they had reached the Northern Sea, General Giant Whale breathed a sigh of relief.

Gazing out at the distant ocean, General Giant Whale spoke with a hint of excitement.

“If we swim northeast for two or three more days at most, we will be at the Dragon Mother’s Dragon Rock Island! ”

Hearing this, Ying Ruoli also visibly grew agitated. Ji Yuan could see her instinctively clenching the hem of her skirt.

“Now we should think about that evil flood dragon. You only know he’s from the Desolate Sea, but you know nothing about whether he’s made any friends or powerful connections. We still need to ask your Dragon Mother about this. ”

“Yes, Mr. Ji is right! ”

Though the General Giant Whale agreed obediently, he was inwardly very excited. That evil flood dragon was doomed for sure, bound to die no matter what!

Though already exhausted, General Giant Whale paid no mind to his condition, instead accelerating once more, swimming furiously towards Dragon Rock Island.

Two days later, the utterly exhausted General Giant Whale sank to the seabed, opening his maw wide to frantically suck in large schools of fish, shrimp, and crabs to replenish his strength.

Once the General Giant Whale had recovered slightly, he dragged Ji Yuan and Ying Ruoli forward for another half-day, until an island shrouded in dark clouds appeared in their sight, lightning occasionally striking its surface.

“Dragon Rock Island? Why are the thunderclouds not dispersing? ”

Ying Ruoli was half excited, half confused.

“It’s alright, it’s alright. Your Highness Lady Ruoli, please rest assured. Seeing these dark clouds means everything is fine. The Dragon Mother has been vexed beyond endurance by the disturbances these past years, always brooding in anger. When she gets angry, thunderclouds gather above Dragon Rock Island. They only thunder, without rain! ”

Ji Yuan looked at General Giant Whale beneath his feet. To conjure thunder out of anger, she certainly could not be weak among those of the dragon lineage, could she?

“Your Dragon Mother seems quite formidable. Why doesn’t she deal with that harassing evil flood dragon herself? ”

“Mr. Ji, the Dragon Mother is indeed formidable, but that evil flood dragon is no slouch either. By the rules of the dragon lineage, if…”

Ying Ruoli, knowing Ji Yuan was unfamiliar with such matters, blushed slightly as she spoke.

“According to the ancient rules passed down among flood dragons and those of the dragon lineage, in such entangling circumstances, if Mother were to personally fight that evil flood dragon, everything would be fine if she won, but if she were to lose… then…”

Ji Yuan quickly raised a hand to stop the Dragon Daughter from continuing.

“I understand, I understand. Wait… then you came here to fight that evil flood dragon? ”

Ying Ruoli quickly waved her hand and shook her head, her head bobbing like a rattle drum.1

“I’m different, I’m different! I’m essentially here for revenge, completely unrelated to the ancient rules. At most, if I lost, it would mean my death. And I won’t lose! Not to mention, with Uncle Ji’s Green Vine Sword, even with my own cultivation alone, I’m still unbeatable! ”

The Dragon Daughter gradually calmed down. Her words were extremely confident, but she indeed had the skills to back such confidence.

“Let’s go see my mother first! ”

“Yes! ”

The General Giant Whale dove underwater and swam towards the base of Dragon Rock Island. Before even approaching the island, they discovered a small group of oddly shaped aquatic spirits blocking their path. The leader was covered in fish scales but had a human-like appearance, with a long tail stretching behind his back, clearly having already refined his horizontal bone.

“Halt! This is Dragon Rock Island, where my Master and Mistress reside. Do not approach!”

Before the General Giant Whale could speak, Ying Ruoli narrowed her eyes, reached out, and instantly grabbed the creature who had spoken, gripping him by the neck and coldly questioning him.

“Your Master? What’s his name? Where is he? ”

“Ah… you, aah, how dare. . . My Master is… Dragon Hua…”

Seeing that Ying Ruoli was about to strangle the creature in anger, Ji Yuan quickly guided a wisp of Sword Qi, flicking his finger to shoot it into the spirit monster’s nape. The latter cried out in pain, its eyes rolled back, and it fainted.

“Keep him to lead the way. Deal with the rest. ”

As Ji Yuan’s words fell, the General Giant Whale chuckled, and opened his colossal maw, inhaling fiercely once more.

The spirit monsters, still in a daze, flailed wildly in the water, trying to escape, but ultimately all fell into the giant whale’s mouth. A sound of bones being crushed echoed, signaling that they had become nourishment for the General Giant Whale.

Beneath Dragon Rock Island was an underwater palace, mostly hidden within hollow stone caverns beneath the island. The arrival of the General Giant Whale brought the the aquatic creatures of Dragon Rock Island immense joy. Two fish maids, delighted, rushed outside Dragon Mother’s pavilion.

“Reporting to Her Majesty the Dragon Mother, General Giant Whale has returned! ”

Inside the dwelling, a woman in blue robes also rose from the side of the bed.

“Did he really contact Mo Rong? ”

“We haven’t seen Master Mo, Dragon Mother. We will know if we go out and see!”

The fish maid’s voice was filled with joy, yet it also caused a slight unease in the woman’s heart. Had that fellow truly arrived?

As she stepped out of the palace in the stone cave, she saw General Giant Whale’s massive body laying prostrate on the seabed. Standing before him was a man and a woman. The man was unfamiliar, but the woman, from her demeanor and her appearance, bore many similarities to herself.

Both mother and daughter were stunned for a while.

“You are… Ruoli? ”

“Mother! I am indeed Ruoli, I’ve come…”

Ying Ruoli spoke in a low voice, her steps hesitant as she approached the Dragon Mother.

“Mother… how have you been these past two hundred years? ”

“You’ve truly grown… Where is your brother? How have you both been? ”

Ying Ruoli was overjoyed. Her mother cared about her and her brother, indicating she wasn’t truly heartless. She quickly took a few steps closer, wanting to grasp the Dragon Mother’s hand. The latter instinctively flinched, but the Dragon Daughter disregarded it and directly embraced her mother.

“We’ve been very well, but we’ve missed Mother dearly! ”

At this, the Dragon Mother’s hands hovered at her sides, unsure of whether or not to lift them. Finally, she too embraced Ying Ruoli.

From the side, Ji Yuan saw that although the Dragon Daughter’s head was lowered, she was clearly smiling very happily. This single embrace, perhaps, made the entire journey worthwhile for her.

“Please rest assured, Mother. As long as that evil flood dragon dares to appear, I will cut him down. If he doesn’t appear, I will seek him out and deal with him properly! ”

Just as the Dragon Mother was about to speak, the Dragon Daughter continued.

“Your daughter is not one to be ungrateful. Besides having confidence in my own cultivation, I’ve also brought help. This here is Uncle Ji, my father’s close friend, a Taoist immortal of profound spiritual power and a deep understanding of the Tao. If I borrow Uncle Ji’s immortal sword, no evil flood dragon can stand in my way! ”

“A friend of Ying Hong’s? ”

Only then did the Dragon Mother turn her solemn gaze to Ji Yuan.

Ji Yuan also found it awkward to address her as Madame Ying, in case she took offense. Calling her by name directly also seemed inappropriate. He could only bow with cupped hands in greeting.

“Indeed, I am Ji Yuan! ”

Ji Yuan possessed neither the aura of an immortal or that of a demon, as if he were merely a mortal. Yet, his very presence standing in the seabed under the water was abnormal, indicating his profound cultivation.

“Did Ying Hong send you? ”

Ji Yuan smiled.

“Naturally, it was Old Master Ying who requested it. He himself actually wished to come, but feared you wouldn’t welcome him, so he held back. But he worried Ruoli wouldn’t succeed, so he specifically asked me to come and assist! ”

Ying Ruoli turned her head slightly to look at Ji Yuan in surprise, only to find her Uncle Ji raising a subtle eyebrow at her.

“Yes, yes, yes! That’s exactly it. Uncle Ji is incredibly powerful and loves to travel the world. My influence couldn’t possibly be that great. It was entirely because Father stepped forward that Uncle Ji agreed to take cation! ”

“Hmph! ”

The Dragon Mother gave a cold snort and said nothing further.
