Also called pellet drums

Chapter 339: Like Boiling Oil Meeting A Raging Fire

General Giant Whale, listening from a slight distance, was overjoyed. He had thought only the Dragon Daughter was coming, but unexpectedly it seemed the Dragon Lord also intended to come. With that, there was even less to fear.

Thus, General Giant Whale opened his mouth and spat out the monster he had been holding, which slid directly next to Ji Yuan.

The Dragon Mother frowned, looking at the Giant Whale.

“Who is this?”

“Reporting to Her Majesty, this is a subordinate of that evil flood dragon, who was lurking suspiciously around Dragon Rock Island. I also ate some other pieces of scum!”

“Then why didn’t you eat him too?”

Ying Ruoli smiled.

“Mother, don’t worry. I deliberately kept him here so he could lead us to the evil flood dragon’s lair, so we can justly slay him! Mother, do you know if that evil flood dragon has any kin or accomplices?”

The Dragon Mother thought for a moment.

“When I first encountered that fellow back then, I didn’t see any other formidable demons by his side. A few years ago, I heard he had been asking around for quite a while before he started persistently harassing me, even eating some of my attendants who ventured out from my aquatic palace. I’m not sure when he found out I resided on Dragon Rock Island.”

General Giant Whale chimed in from outside,

“That evil flood dragon came from the Desolate Sea. What kind of foundation could he have here? Even if he made some acquaintances, they would be nothing but fair-weather friends. They’d show him respect while he’s alive, but who would mourn him once he’s dead? Even if some came along from the Desolate Sea, with the savage nature of that place, no one there would care. Besides…

“If we can reveal the Dragon Lord’s identity, hmph, don’t the True Dragons of the Four Seas all know each other? Who wouldn’t afford some respect to the Dragon Lord? At this point, who among the underwater beings would dare to lend him aid?”

There was indeed some truth to his words, but the Dragon Mother was clearly displeased, so Ying Ruoli quickly changed the subject.

“Alright, alright. Let’s deal with that mixed-color flood dragon first. Uncle Ji, how should we interrogate him?”

Ji Yuan thought for a moment, then said:

“Just let him go; he will lead us there himself.”

With that, Ji Yuan directly picked up the demon on the ground and violently flung him outwards. With a gentle push of force, it left a trail in the water as it was sent flying all the way to the edge of Dragon Rock Island.

It seemed some kind of technique had been applied to him when he had been thrown, as the demon soon awoke.

The demon looked left and right, discovering that all its companions were gone. It then glanced towards Dragon Rock Island, where a large whale occasionally swished its tail, but assumed that he had been knocked out and then simply ignored.

‘Oh no! I must quickly inform Dragon Lord Hua! ’

The fish-like monster shuddered, immediately realizing it needed to run. So it quickly swam and maneuvered in the water, skimming along the seabed towards the west.

A short while after he left, Ji Yuan and the Dragon Daughter followed after him. The Dragon Mother hesitated for a moment but ultimately joined them, still carried by General Giant Whale, though this time their movements were concealed by the Dragon Daughter’s spiritual techniques.

Approximately nine hundred li off the shore, in a sea trench, lay an underwater palace built of rocks and coral. Though crude, it had been well-maintained over the years.

The spirit monster’s swimming speed was far inferior that of General Giant Whale; it took the better part of a day to cover the nine hundred li.

At this moment, in what could barely pass for a main hall, two figures sat enthroned: one, a man in a dark-green robe wearing a tall hairpin crown, and the other, a bald, bare-chested man with grayish-brown skin. The other seats were occupied by various water spirits, some more human-like, others resembling fish or shrimp, all boisterously eating and drinking together.

“Come, come, Brother Shark! You honor me with your visit today. Let’s not discuss demands for now. I shall consider them and surely give you a satisfactory answer. Come, let’s drink, let’s drink! This is fine human wine I acquired recently, after sinking a dozen ships while traveling through the Northern Sea near the Heng Continent. There are also some other things, too, like spices and preserved fruits; those mortals know how to enjoy themselves better than we do!”

The green-robed man seemed in extremely high spirits, laughing heartily as he continuously explained the various items.

The gray-skinned, bare-chested man raised his wine cup with a smile, looked at the bubbly liquid protected by a bubble, and took a shallow sip.

‘Hmph, what an uncouth inexperienced bumpkin!’

However, he still spoke with a hearty laugh.

“The wine is indeed good, but since Brother Hua sank over a dozen ships, what about the people on board? Tsk-tsk, human flesh is much more…”

“Uh… Hahahaha, Brother Hui,1 you know I can’t control my mouth! Besides, if people die and soak in seawater, the taste changes!”

“Oh, oh, it was just a slip of the tongue, I’m just being nosy. If Brother Hua has time in the future, I’ll take you to a good place. Although the journey is a bit far, it will definitely be unforgettable!”

The green-robed man’s interest was immediately piqued.

“Oh? Where is that?”

“Of course, it’s the Black…”

The gray-skinned man’s words were cut short as an extremely flustered voice came from outside.

“Master! Master. . . this is bad, Master!”

The green-robed man stood up. Not long after, the fish-scaled spirit monster stumbled into the hall in a rush, both swimming and running at the same time.

“Hmm? Weren’t you supposed to be watching that beauty? Why are you back by yourself?”

The fish-scaled spirit monster bowed to the green-robed man.

“It wasn’t that this humble one abandoned my post, but that ferocious Giant Whale has returned, and he’s brought reinforcements! I fear… I fear it will be unfavorable for you, Sir. This humble one only managed to escape after a fierce struggle, and all my other brothers were eaten…”

“Hahahaha…”

The green-robed man burst into loud laughter.

“You managed to fight them, yet they dare to seek trouble with me? I’ve always been tolerant of them, and though I’ve eaten many servants from that beauty’s residence, I never laid a hand on that whale. But he has dared put himself on the line again and again. If that is the case, they cannot blame me for acting!”

“Brother Hua, have you encountered trouble? Do you need my help?”

The gray-skinned man, still seated and drinking, asked, though his intent was more symbolic than practical, as he didn’t even bother to stand up.

“Ah, Brother Hui, no need to mind it. Although that Giant Whale is powerful, he’s just a small fry who hasn’t even fully transformed yet.”

“But didn’t your subordinate say he brought reinforcements?”

The gray-skinned man casually asked.

“Hahaha. . . Brother Hui, have you forgotten? Back then, you said you wanted the soul of an aquatic monster with profound and pure cultivation. As a token of my sincerity, I gathered many servants, along with a few of your brothers, and we went to the Eastern Sea to ambush and hunt down a black flood dragon!”

The gray-skinned man frowned.

“I remember now. Although I didn’t go, it’s said that black flood dragon had an extremely profound cultivation. Despite the ambush and unexpected trap, it still managed to escape. We only got a dragon tendon. His dragon soul and dragon body naturally got away. With so many ghosts and gods on the Eastern Cloud Continent, we didn’t dare to rashly pursue it… Could there be anything special about this black flood dragon?”

“Haha, actually, there’s one thing I didn’t fully explain. I previously ate many of the beauty’s servants, and extracted some information from them. They have a backer–that that black flood dragon, whom they all called ‘Master Mo.’ He must have been the beauty’s former lover. But even if that black flood dragon hadn’t died back then, he wouldn’t have amounted to much, certainly not a reliable ally.”

The green-robed man said this with a satisfied air, causing the gray-skinned man to realize something and inadvertently look at him with higher regard. He hadn’t expected this fellow to possess such cunning; he wasn’t completely brainless.

“You vile, monstrous demon! So Mo Rong’s death was connected to you!”

Ying Ruoli’s clear, cold voice drifted in from outside.

Immediately following was a deafening roar as the outer hall of the underwater palace was directly obliterated as General Giant Whale slammed himself into it. The spot where the green-robed man and his gray-skinned guest had been standing shook violently, with rocks and coral continuously falling.

The green-robed man’s fury surged. Dragon Qi surged forth, his expression contorted with age, and he leapt out of the hall.

“Who wishes to die? How dare you cause trouble at Lord Hua’s abode!”

After the man finished cursing, he saw a giant whale outside, with two women and one man on its back.

“Beauties? You, and a younger sister? Excellent! You’ve all delivered yourselves right to my doorstep!”

The green-robed man intently stared at the exquisite faces and shapely figures of the Dragon Mother and Dragon Daughter for a long time. Though their expressions were cold, that didn’t matter. He then looked at the green-robed man beside them.

“You are Mo Rong? Still not dead? Indeed, there’s hardly any Dragon Qi left in you!”

Ji Yuan shook his head, too lazy to even speak. He extended his left hand flat, and the Green Vine Sword automatically flew into it. Then, he gently ran his right hand over the blade before handing it to Ying Ruoli.

The Dragon Mother, who had kept her head held high, lowered it slightly. Standing atop the massive whale’s head, she looked down at the green-robed man with an air of superiority.

“Hua Hou, this is my daughter. She’s here to do something for me!”

The multi-colored flood dragon named Hua Hou froze for a moment, then looked at Ying Ruoli.

“Your daughter? To do what?”

Ying Ruoli channeled all her spiritual energy into her hands, then focused on the Green Vine Sword. She narrowed her eyes and blurted out her answer.

“To kill you!”

At the same moment she spoke, the Dragon Daughter already reached out to draw the sword, but as she gripped the hilt and pulled, she felt an immense strain, as if wielding thousands of pounds of weight.

‘So heavy! ’

Her right arm trembled almost imperceptibly as she mustered all her strength, but with a final burst of effort she drew the immortal sword.

“Shiiiing–“

The sword rang out.

Swoosh…

A streak of silver-white light flashed across the seabed, slicing diagonally across Hua Hou’s face as his pupils constricted violently and he instinctively dodged.

Boom, rumble, rumble, rumble…

Massive chunks of rock collapsed into the trench, nearly half of the underwater palace was destroyed. An even deeper sword mark was left at the bottom of the trench.

But as Ying Ruoli unleashed that sword strike, she inwardly cursed. She couldn’t fully control the immortal sword after all; she had missed slightly, only severing one of his arms and one leg, and scraping off a small patch of his flesh.

“Roooar…”

Hua Hou’s pained dragon cry rang out. Amidst the endless blood and murky sediment, a multicolored flood dragon revealed its true form, writhing in agony in the sea trench, looking exactly like an earthworm freshly pulled from the soil.

“Roooar… Roo… Roo… Rooooar…”

Ji Yuan saw Ying Ruoli still intended to use her spiritual energy to wield the sword, and extended a hand to stop her.

“Though not dead, he is gravely wounded. There’s no need to waste spiritual energy.”

Of course, more importantly, there was no need to waste the Green Vine Sword’s Sword Qi. Although the Dragon Daughter’s spiritual energy replenished a large portion of the energy, without the immortal sword’s own Sword Qi, it wouldn’t possess that unparalleled sharpness.

However, Ji Yuan naturally wouldn’t let everyone just stand there. Instead, he bent his right thumb, and with his four fingers, he lightly waved twice in front of his chest like fanning himself. Then he casually opened his mouth and exhaled a large plume of red-gray flames, which flew towards the multicolored flood dragon, who was being tormented and driven mad by pain in the sea trench.

The Dragon Daughter, the Dragon Mother, General Giant Whale, the aquatic creatures fleeing from the surrounding structures, and that gray-skinned man who had just come out all watched.

That peculiar-colored plume of fire, astonishingly, never extinguished even deep beneath the sea, and finally landed on the multicolored flood dragon.

Whoosh-whoosh-whoosh…

Despite being beneath the sea, the entire body of the flood dragon, which had just been shrouded in dust and gore, instantly erupted in a fierce, red-gray blaze, like boiling oil meeting a raging fire.

“Roooar…”

The flood dragon’s cry suddenly rose several notches.

‘What?! Fire control? Burning a flood dragon at the bottom of the sea?!’

The gray-skinned man had already inwardly cursed when he saw the Dragon Daughter wielding the immortal sword. Now, witnessing this eerie scene of fire control, his scalp tingled, and his whole body trembled.

Just as he was about to sneak away, he inadvertently glanced up towards the Giant Whale and found that everyone was either dumbfounded or staring at the raging fire with exaggerated expressions. Only the one who had set the fire, holding the sword, was facing his direction.

Suddenly, he felt as if his entire body was being pricked by needles.

His name is Hui (灰 huī: ash/gray/dust) and he has gray skin, LOL

Chapter 340: Best Not to Interfere

Fortunately, this feeling of being watched was only fleeting, before he was obstructed by the reddish-grey flames and the surrounding dust.

The grey-skinned man hesitated for a moment, cautiously lowering his body and transforming into a small shark no longer than an arm’s length. The small shark swam through the murky waters, fleeing towards the end of the trench.

The other aquatic monsters were frantically swimming upwards, while this small shark managed to escape by using them as a distraction. The water temperature at the bottom of the trench was rapidly rising at this moment, and normal aquatic creatures simply couldn’t withstand it for long.

The True Fire of Samadhi was sometimes as cold as ice, and sometimes incredibly scorching. Today, as it burned the flood dragon, it manifested outwardly in its Yang form, sweeping forth with overwhelming heat.

After swimming for more than ten li at a moderate pace within this great trench, the shark finally swam along the cliff face and ascended to the seabed above, then swam outwards with the surrounding schools of fish, taking care throughout this entire process to conceal its aura.

Looking back towards the trench, some seabed dust was only just now swirling upwards from below, and the firelight had vanished. It wasn’t clear whether the fire had finally been extinguished by the seawater, or… whether that unlucky flood dragon, Hua Hou, who also called himself Dragon Lord Hua, had already been completely incinerated.

The shark dared not dare to linger; it shivered in the water and vigorously flicked its tail, swimming a little faster.

…

In the depths of the trench, Ying Ruoli and the Dragon Mother personally watched as Flood Dragon Hua was consumed by the reddish-grey flames, until there was nothing left to burn. Only then did the flames gradually subside.

Despite being deep within the trench, surrouned by the powerful and icy cold waters of the Northern Sea, the fire remained completely unaffected.

Well, the surroundings were now filled with bubbles; that was the result of the rapidly rising temperature of the seawater forcing out dissolved gases causing them to boil. In other words, the seawater in the depths of the trench was already perfectly hot enough to make fish soup.

General Giant Whale could only barely keep himself from retreating at this moment; staying here was extremely uncomfortable. If it were an ordinary whale that hadn’t gained sentience, he would probably be cooked medium-rare by now.

The Dragon Mother subconsciously glanced at Ji Yuan a few more times. Since this man was that fellow’s closest friend, he was indeed no ordinary immortal cultivator; this fire-controlling technique was simply astonishing.

“Uncle Ji, that was your True Fire of Samadhi just now, wasn’t it?”

It was Ying Ruoli’s first time seeing Ji Yuan display a fire-control technique, but she could immediately guess what it was.

Ji Yuan looked at Ying Ruoli, then his gaze turned towards the top of the trench.

“Did Gao Tianming tell you?”

“He mentioned it to my brother, then my father and I also learned of it.”

Ji Yuan nodded.

“It was indeed the True Fire of Samadhi. Once this fire takes form, it is considerably powerful. This multicolored flood dragon, if it hadn’t been injured, it might have been able to withstand the True Fire with his own spiritual energy for a while. But it was first wounded by a sword, and then lost composure due to the pain, and was directly set alight by the True Fire.”

As he spoke, Ji Yuan reached out towards the embers, where a trace of residual fire still lingered, sweeping his sleeve and making a beckoning gesture. From within the ashes, a dragon pearl flew out, still wreathed in wisps of True Fire of Samadhi.

It was precisely because of this dragon pearl that the True Fire had not yet extinguished.

A dragon pearl was considered a supreme treasure of dragons and flood dragons. Those whose cultivation was not yet deep enough might never be able to cultivate one. Losing a dragon pearl was a significant loss, almost equivalent to losing half one’s life and nearly half one’s cultivation.

Unexpectedly, this multicolored flood dragon actually possessed a dragon pearl.

The True Fire of Samadhi that was wrapped around the dragon pearl automatically extinguished as it reached Ji Yuan’s hand, leaving only a dragon pearl the size of a goose egg.

Before, every time Ji Yuan wielded the True Fire of Samadhi, he had been extremely cautious, fearing that he might spontaneously combust one day. But now, after achieving the Five Elements Returning to Origin, he could wield it with ease equal to his other techniques, appearing even more effortless.

After the True Fire of Samadhi vanished, the dragon pearl began to emit light again, a halo of light spreading out about two zhang.

Looking at the beautiful dragon pearl, Ji Yuan subconsciously remarked.

“This dragon pearl is pleasing to the eye, but it’s not very big.”

The dragon pearl, like its dragon owner, could change size, but this pearl was from Hua Hou in his true form, so this was considered its largest state. Although the size of a goose-egg seemed quite large from Ji Yuan’s perspective, it still seemed too small when compared to a flood dragon over tens of zhang long, while the range of its radiance was just barely acceptable.

“Uncle Ji, this dragon pearl is already quite large. I didn’t expect this multicolored flood dragon to still have such a large dragon pearl.”

As the Dragon Girl spoke, she glanced around.

“Oh, right, Uncle Ji, who was the creature that multicolored flood dragon was entertaining earlier? He must certainly be related to Mo Rong’s death; why hasn’t he come out after all this time?”

“He’s already run away.”

Ji Yuan’s words surprised Ying Ruoli slightly; she thought that with Ji Yuan around, the other party definitely wouldn’t be able to escape, so she hadn’t paid special attention to him earlier. However, immediately realized something.

“Uncle Ji, are you also planning to see where he went?”

Ji Yuan put away the dragon pearl and looked up again at the top of the trench, and answered in a calm voice.

“That wasn’t exactly my intention. I originally planned to intimidate him into submission.”

“Ah? Then why…”

Ying Ruoli stopped halfway through her sentence when she saw Ji Yuan shake his head slightly.

“Let’s go. Let’s return to Dragon Rock Island first.”

The Dragon Mother watched as the multicolored flood dragon turned to ash, without even a chance for its dragon soul to escape; the pent-up frustration in her heart dissipated considerably.

“Yes, Sir!”

General Giant Whale General replied and hastily swung his body upwards. This dreadful place was now unbearably hot, the temperature now so high that he couldn’t stay any longer. There were still some monsters around that hadn’t managed to escape and were already cooked, but he didn’t dare open his mouth to eat them now, fearing the boiling water would scald him.

Upon returning to Dragon Rock Island, General Giant Whale was already eager to announce the good news to everyone.

He roared loudly.

“We have eliminated that evil multicolored flood dragon! From now on, Dragon Rock Island will no longer have to endure humiliation!”

The Giant Whale’s roar echoed throughout Dragon Rock Island’s underwater palace, and the aquatic creatures within secretly celebrated.

Now, not only General Giant Whale and a few of Dragon Mother’s personal fish-maids, but also many of Dragon Rock Island’s aquatic creatures now knew how powerful the Dragon Mother’s relatives were, and all their previous trepidation vanished.

Before long, Dragon Rock Island began to prepare a celebratory banquet. It wasn’t a grand feast, nor would anyone be invited; it was just the aquatic creatures of the underwater palace celebrating together among themselves.

The Dragon Mother and Dragon Daughter conversed together in a room, likely confiding their concerns and affection for each other.

Meanwhile, Ji Yuan was alone in the hall, sampling Dragon Rock Island’s snacks, accompanied only by a few fish maids and monsters. General Giant Whale had wanted to accompany him, but his body was too large, he simply couldn’t squeeze in, so he went out to forage for food.

Ji Yuan tasted each type of pastry, and only ate what suited his palate.

After their initial nervousness, the fish maids beside him could now chat a bit with Ji Yuan.

“Mr. Ji, you came from the Eastern Cloud Continent? It’s so far, you must be tired, right?”

“It’s not very close by, but the one who must be tired is General Giant Whale; he carried us all the way here.”

“Oh.”

A fish maid quickly refilled Ji Yuan’s conch cup with a drink.

“Mr. Ji, you and the Dragon Lord are good friends. Is he fierce?”

Ji Yuan considered for a moment before replying.

“Whether he’s fierce or not depends on the person and the situation. Under normal circumstances, apart from having high standards, his temper is quite alright.”

“Oh, then I’m relieved.”

“Mhm, me too, me too!”

“That’s right, Miss Ruoli has such good temper, as does Mr. Ji, so the Dragon Lord must also have a good temper.”

Just as they were about to ask something else, Ji Yuan stood up.

“If anyone asks later, just say I have stepped out for a moment and will return shortly.”

With that, Ji Yuan stepped out as if treading on flowing water, exiting the underater palace directly, then rose upwards towards the sea surface.

Before long, a wave broke open on the ocean surface near Dragon Rock Island, and Ji Yuan flew out from it, stepping onto the island as if treading on air.

“How was it?”

Ji Yuan asked, seemingly out of the blue.

A thin mist lingered over the island. Soon, a faint silhouette of a dragon emerged from within it, and the Old Dragon’s voice drifted over through the fog.

“I have been following him a long time, observing his escape path. On the surface, it appears to have no connection with the True Dragon of the Northern Sea. I have already planted illusory Qi in his body, consuming most of the energy of this illusory dragon projection. It will soon dissipate.”

Upon hearing this, Ji Yuan stared at him intently.

“Then why did you call me here to meet you? Hurry and go see your wife!”

The old dragon looked at Ji Yuan without speaking. Presumably, if it hadn’t been Ji Yuan, even his Dragon Son or Daughter would have been lectured or even scolded, but since it was Ji Yuan before him, the old dragon could only change the subject.

“Mr. Ji’s True Fire of Samadhi is truly extraordinary. Sometimes I even wonder how many more unknown skills you have, how many more heaven-defying techniques you possess that even contemporary immortal cultivators have never seen?”

Ji Yuan replied courteously.

“Likewise. Isn’t the Great Illusion Technique just as wondrous? I didn’t even notice it.”

“You really didn’t notice?”

The old dragon’s misty dragon shadow moved a little closer, carefully observing the expression on Ji Yuan’s face, only to see the latter let out a rare chuckle.

“No, I didn’t notice, but I could guess through deduction.”

The dragon silhouette turned to look westward and sighed.

“Mo Rong’s death back then was indeed related to the Black Wasteland. I just didn’t expect it wouldn’t be from the Northern Sea, and not the area around Dazhen. Hmph, then those demons I killed back then died for the sake of the demons here, didn’t they?”

Ji Yuan replied calmly.

“Few of them were innocent.”

“That’s also true!”

The dragon silhouette looked at Ji Yuan again and clasped its two front claws together as a gesture of respect.

“Mr. Ji, I am once again in your debt. Thank you for avenging Mo Rong. As for what happens next, Mr. Ji, there is no need to concern yourself with it.”

Ji Yuan also returned the gesture. The Old Dragon was someone who valued his reputation, so Ji Yuan naturally granted him this grace.

“Alright, in that case, I won’t interfere.”

The misty dragon silhouette completely dissipated. Ji Yuan didn’t know if the old dragon’s illusory form had truly vanished or if it still lingered.

His gaze turned back to the east; there were matters to attend to on the Eastern Cloud Continent as well. Hopefully, it would be before the so-called Cultivator’s Grand Assembly.

T/n: JY casually makes fish soup at the bottom of the ocean as his friends watch on in horror

In case you didn’t quite get that ending, the Old Dragon is basically deeply thanking Ji Yuan for finding these evil guys and saying “leave the rest to me” as a way of making up for his past failures in catching Mo Rong’s murderers and saving face.

Chapter 341: The Hunters’ Harvest Festival on Niu Kui Mountain

Ji Yuan was in no hurry to return. Instead, he observed the surface of the waters around Dragon Rock Island. The island itself was not too small. It was lush with vegetation, though many spots bore scorch marks from lightning strikes. Of course, at the moment, the weather around the island was sunny and calm.

After looking around for a while, he still couldn’t figure out why the island was named Dragon Rock Island; perhaps it was just a name someone made up on the spot.1 With that complete, Ji Yuan then flicked his finger a couple times towards the beach.

One by one, green coconuts fell from some of the coconut trees in that direction and rolled to Ji Yuan’s feet. The waters of the Northern Sea waters were clearly colder than those of the Eastern Sea, yet unexpectedly, Dragon Rock Island had thriving coconut trees — a sight he had never witnessed in Dazhen nor in Zuyue.

Behind him, another wave broke the surface of the ocean, and a current lifted Ying Ruoli out of the water.

“Uncle Ji, are you picking coconuts on the island? Wouldn’t it be better to have the servants do this? “

Ji Yuan didn’t reveal the truth. After picking about twenty coconuts, he gathered them up and went back into the water, asking a different question to change the subject.

“I just came over for a walk and picked them along the way when I saw them. So how did it go, did your mother agree to return to the Tongtian River? “

This was the goal Ying Ruoli had set before she arrived, so Ji Yuan was certain she must have mentioned it earlier.

Ying Ruoli shook her head.

“Mother absolutely refuses to go to the Tongtian River. “

Even so, the Dragon Daughter still smiled.

“However, I persuaded her to go to the Eastern Sea. When the time comes, I’ll ask my elder brother to help me find a suitable temporary home for Mother there, so it will be easier to look after her.”

“That’s good. This place is, after all, a bit too remote. “

Though Ji Yuan verbally agreed, he privately thought that the Dragon Mother was no delicate woman; the lightning scorch marks around Dragon Rock Island alone were evidence of that.

Upon arriving at the underwater palace of Dragon Rock Island, Ji Yuan felt a bit awkward bringing out the twenty coconuts, so he simply tucked them into his sleeve. Ying Ruoli, of course, didn’t say anything, merely stating that Uncle Ji had gone out for a stroll.

The so-called banquet at Dragon Rock Island was indeed lavish. However, there was one thing that Ji Yuan found slightly unusual: almost everything was raw—fresh fish, fresh shrimp, fresh shellfish, all had been filleted, neatly sliced, and served on exquisite platters.

Ji Yuan had never been fond of sashimi in his past life, and had rarely had similar dishes in this one, but after politely sampling some, he found the taste surprisingly good.

…

At the foot of Niu Kui Mountain in Ji Province of Dazhen, in a village nestled against the mountainside, the villagers were just beginning to stir.

A burly man, dressed in thick early-spring clothing, finished his steaming hot breakfast, gathered his bow and arrows, long spear, and rope, and stepped out of his courtyard.

The man surveyed the cooking smoke rising from both ends of the village, then planted his spear into the ground, cupped his hands around his mouth, and bellowed loudly.

“Mountain hunters, we’re heading out!”

His voice echoed through the village for a while, then he repeated his words.

Before long, people emerged from many houses in the village. Most carried bows and arrows, as well as long staves or pitchforks. A few carried ropes.

Over a dozen men gathered, of all sizes and statures.

As they reached the village gate, their families came to see them off, and everyone bid them farewell.

“Don’t worry, our luck has been good lately. And with the weather so cold, we won’t be going deep into the mountains, just checking traps around the outer perimeter. “

“Husband, come back soon! Dinner is waiting for you!”

“Honey, the mountain paths are treacherous in this cold. Whether we have a harvest festival or not doesn’t matter;we still haven’t finished the meat from last winter!”

“Alright, alright, I know, I know. “

“Go back, everyone, it’s still cold outside! “

The group took a few steps forward, turned back, and called out one last time. Once they entered the mountain, their pace quickened.

The leader rubbed his hands together, then took the long spear from his back to use as a walking stick.

“Let’s go check the traps first!”

Having grown up in the mountains, scrambling around like wild monkeys as children, the cold weather and thick clothing had little impact on these hunters. Their steps remained nimble, and even a martial arts expert would find it difficult to rival their skill.

After walking in the mountains for over an hour, they reached the first pre-set trap. The leader was still a dozen steps away when he peered through the gaps in the trees and saw that the camouflage covering the trap had collapsed. A smile crept onto his lips.

“It’s very likely something fell fell in! “

Soon, more than a dozen men gathered around the edge of the trap.

“Wow, a wild boar!”

“Not bad, not bad, lots of meat, I like it! “

“Haha, but its hide isn’t worth much…”

“It’s good enough to have something. “

Watching the wild boar charge around and strike the sides of the pit after catching sight of them, the hunters’ faces lit up with joy.

“Leave it for now, let’s go check the other traps! “

The leader waved his hand, and the group set off again, checking one trap after another. They had set a total of ten traps in different locations, including both pitfall traps and net traps. Five out of the first nine traps had game, which was a very high success rate indeed.

Even before they reached the last trap, the hunters were already laughing and chatting quietly.

“Roar…”

A distant roar instantly silenced the group. However, no one panicked; everyone instantly crouched low.

The leader gestured with a hand, signaling. Those behind him grabbed their bows or spears. Others untied what had appeared to be ropes from their backs, unfurling them to reveal two large nets.

“What’s the last trap? A net or a pitfall? “

“A pitfall. “

The leader nodded, waved his hand, and pointed to the ground. The hunters understood, and stealthily made their way forward.

They were all experienced hunters; the wind direction was in their favor, and as long as they made little noise, they had a good chance.

The sound suggested a fierce beast. Though the risk was higher, they outnumbered it and weren’t too afraid. Plus, the hides of wild beasts were very valuable.

When they reached a few dozen paces from the pitfall, they peered through the trees, and saw that a sound was indeed coming from within, accompanied by the sounds of chewing, gnawing, and tearing of flesh and bone.

The leader thought for a moment, then pointed to seven or eight of the men. About half of them held bows and arrows, and stayed put to provide support. Then he suddenly waved his hand.

The next moment, all the men he had pointed to rushed towards the pit trap, shouting loudly to intimidate the beast.

“Aaaaaaaaaahhhhhh!”

“Come on, aaaaaaaaaaah! “

“Charge! Aaaaaaaaahhhhhhh! “

…

“Roar, roar…”

A panicked roar came inside the pit. After a moment of hesitation, a mottled, patterned shadow suddenly leaped out of the trap, only to run straight into a large net of thick ropes that had been cast over it.

“Roar…”

“Hahahahaha… a big leopard! “

“This pelt is worth a fortune, hahahahaha…”

“Not bad, not bad. Tiger or leopard hides are rare treasures; I can go to town and buy my wife a piece of patterned floral fabric with this! “

In contrast to the panicked leopard, the hunters were overjoyed.

After laughing, the leader couldn’t help but say,

“Since last winter, there have been many more wild animals on the periphery of the mountain, and they’ve been exceptionally easy to catch. “

“Hey, isn’t that a good thing?”

The leader glanced towards the depths of Niu Kui Mountain.

“It is indeed a good thing, but I keep feeling that some of the animals from deep within Niu Kui Mountain have moved out to the perimeter. “

“Huh? Could it be that there’s no food inside? “

The leader shook his head.

“I don’t know. Niu Kui Mountain is so vast; perhaps it’s just our area that’s like this. In any case, it’s a good thing for us. “

With that, the man joined them in processing the game. The net trapping the leopard had already been gathered into a ball. After pulling the leopard’s four limbs through the mesh and binding them securely, they directly strangled the leopard with a rope. Unless someone had paid for a live capture, fierce beasts were generally not taken out of the mountain alive.

The prey in the pit had already been killed by the leopard, its belly torn open, but the hunters didn’t waste it. The leopard had only eaten the entrails; most of the meat was still there, so they naturally took it with them.

At this moment, the sky suddenly darkened. However, it was only noon, so it couldn’t be nightfall; rather, thunderclouds had gathered.

“Rumble…”

The sound of thunder and the darkening sky startled the hunters.

“Oh no, the weather’s changed again! Quickly, take the game and head back. “

“Let’s go, let’s go! Take everything and go! Don’t reset the traps! “

The group, returning with a full load of spoils, hurried back. Little did they know, similar scenes were unfolding in many places around the vast Niu Kui Mountain.

Dark clouds once again densely blanketed the sky above Niu Kui Mountain, lightning flashing through the thick, rolling, lead-like clouds.

“Rumble…”

“Crack… boom…”

The lightning brilliantly illuminated the depths of the great mountain.

A massive, majestic hairless tiger poked half its body out of its cave, its intricately patterned face gleaming in the lightning. However, it showed no fear. Instead, it gazed intently at the thunderclouds in the sky.

“Honored Master once casually asked, ‘Who controls this thunder? Is it not Heaven itself?’”

Gradually, Lu Shanjun emerged from the cave, his steps seemingly unhurried, yet each of his strides covered a significant distance. He soon arrived at a stone promontory and looked up at the ink-black clouds pressing down from the sky.

“Rumble…”

“ROAR—”

The tiger’s roar instantly drowned out the thunder, accompanying the howling winds that echoed throughout the mountains and wilderness.2

However, the animals in this part of the mountain had long since fled, migrating to the outermost periphery.

“Shanjun, are you going to transform into human form?”

A weak voice came from the side of the stone platform. Lu Shanjun looked over; a red fox’s head was poking out.

“Haha, that depends on when the clouds overhead dare to strike me. I advise you to stay far away from me, or take temporary refuge in my cave. Though you’ve made much progress these years, with your cultivation level, if you were hit by such a lightning strike, you would be severely injured, if not killed.”

Severely injured? Hu Yun looked up at the sky, feeling like Lu Shanjun was overestimating him.

“Then I’ll leave too. Be careful!”

Leaving these words, Hu Yun took off running, not heading for Lu Shanjun’s oppressive cave, but rather directly towards the edge of the mountainside.

Chapter 342: Legend of the Tiger Spirit

“Rumble, rumble, rumble…”

“Roar…”

The tiger’s roar mingled with the sound of thunder, echoing from the depths of Niu Kui Mountain. By the time it reached the outskirts, its volume had significantly diminished, once again drowned out by a new peal of thunder.

The group of hunters, laden with their game and mountain goods, hurried along the path. If a torrential downpour were to start at this crucial moment, the mountain would become wet, slippery, and cold, making the journey exceedingly difficult. They might even be forced to spend the night in the mountains.

“Rumble, rumble, rumble…”

A peculiar echo reverberated. The leader halted, looking back towards the depths of Niu Kui Mountain. In the distance, on the furthest peak his vision could discern, numerous trees and bamboo swayed violently, as if gusts of ominous wind were sweeping out from the depths of the mountains.

“Awooo… roar…”

Another faint sound drifted over, making the leader fall into a strange, hazy hallucination. In his eyes, he seemed to see, far beyond the mountain, a swirl of grey-black mist rising into the sky, forming a massive beast’s head beneath the dark clouds, roaring incessantly at the thunderclouds above.

“Hiss…!”

The scene made the man instinctively gasp, his legs feeling heavy, as though they had been filled with lead, unable to move.

A hunter suddenly noticed their leader wasn’t following them. He turned around, only to find him standing, staring into the depths of the mountains, and quickly asked:

“Boss, what are you looking at? More game? ”

The question startled the man, making him shiver involuntarily. He glanced at the distant sky again, but there was no sign of anything; it was as if it had all been an illusion. He recovered his senses and glanced at the hunter beside him.

“Nothing, nothing! Let’s go, quickly! Even if there was game, we can’t stop. We absolutely cannot spend the night in the mountains! ”

The leader urged them on, and they quickly hurried forward again.

“Who’d want to spend the night here in this cold? ”

“But we hunted so much this time, the goods are heavy! ”

“Run, run! ”

The group of men, laden with game, continuously trekked through the mountains. Fortunately, though the dark clouds grew larger and the thunder became frightfully frequent, the rain ultimately did not immediately begin to fall, allowing the hunters to successfully reach the foot of the mountain.

At the village entrance, a crowd of villagers had already been waiting. Seeing the hunters return, they all enthusiastically cheered.

“Look! They’re back, with so much big game! ”

“Let’s go help. ”

“Look, look! They’re carrying a big tiger in the front! ”

“Oh my goodness, a big tiger? ”

“No, no, don’t speak nonsense, that’s a big leopard! ”

“Jackals, wolves, tigers, and leopards – they’re all pretty much the same.”

“Right, right! They’re all top-grade pelts! ”

The hunters’ families and the curious villagers who had come to watch all streamed out, helping the hunters carry the game. The heavy wild boars and brightly colored beasts looked, in the eyes of those who knew their trade, like succulent meats and furs that could be exchanged for a lot of silver.

“Rumble, rumble, rumble…”

Thunder roared once more, followed by a “drip, drip, drip…” as the torrential downpour finally began. The hunters, their families, and the villagers quickly rushed back to their homes.

The mountain village itself wasn’t particularly large. That very evening, the two wild boars were cooked. The original plan was for every household in the village to receive a portion of the wild boar meat, allowing them to enjoy a feast of fresh wild game at the start of spring.

However, the leader of the hunters had been feeling uneasy ever since their return, so he decided to take some goods and his wife to her parents’ home.

Carrying a rack of pork ribs and a wild boar foreleg, accompanied by his wife and child, the hunter arrived at the home of the old village chief, who was also his father-in-law.

His mother-in-law and his wife brought out steaming wild boar ribs and meat. Before cooking, they had rubbed the meat with spicy wild ginger and wild pepper from the mountains, which largely removing the gamey smell. One foreleg, however, was stewed separately until it was incredibly tender, and placed in a large, ornate bowl.

“Come on, Dad, eat quickly while it’s hot! This foreleg meat is your favorite! ”

“That’s right, Dad, we specifically set this aside for you, stewed until it’s tender.”

His daughter and son-in-law urged him with smiles on their faces.

“Haha, how filial!”

The old village chief chuckled, lifted up his chopsticks, and picked a piece of the boar foreleg, offering it to his young grandson by his side.

“Ah~”

The latter opened his mouth wide and ate the pork in one bite, puffing out his cheeks as he chewed vigorously.

“Sit down, everyone! Let’s eat! ”

The family gathered around and sat down together to being eating. Aside from a pot of radish stew, there were primarily two pots of pork, plus a jar of rice wine. This kind of meal would make even those in Ning’an County green with envy.

“Rumble, rumble, rumble…”

Thunder rumbled once again, but the people inside the house had already grown accustomed to it. They ate the meat and vegetables on the table by the light of an oil lamp.

After eating for a while, the man, under the pretext of pouring wine for his father-in-law, quietly asked the old man.

“Dad, I have a question for you. I remember when I was a child, Niu Kui Mountain seemed to have been plagued by demons for a while? ”

“Oh, why are you bringing that up! ”

The mother-in-law shot her son-in-law a glare, but the child, on the other hand, grew excited.

“Demons? Do we have demons around here? What about gods and immortals? ”

“The gods are all in the temples! Eat your food. ”

The child’s mother answered with a smile, picking up a piece of radish and stuffing it into the child’s mouth. Otherwise, this little one would only eat meat.

“Dad, I faintly remember something like that happening when I was little. Back then, the authorities seemed…”

“That’s right! Something similar did happen back then. ”

The old village chief had been the village chief in this mountain village for nearly forty years. He had a good relationship with every village head in the two surrounding villages, and remembered some important past events.

“Counting how many years have passed, it’s been almost twenty now!”

The old village chief said with a sigh, then drank the rice wine from his small cup.

“Grandpa, Grandpa, tell me about it! ”

The young grandson swallowed the turnip and meat in his mouth, pushed away his mother’s chopsticks, and pleaded. The hunter, his father, quickly and eagerly refilled the old man’s wine and picked up another piece of pork knuckle, with skin and meat.

“Alright, I’ll tell you about it then! ”

The old village chief took a sip of rice wine, put the knuckle into his mouth, and chewed, his eyes squinting as if he had returned to that time of fear and turmoil.

“I can’t recall exactly how long it has been, but I suppose it’s probably been almost twenty years. Back then, your father wasn’t much older than you are now. ”

The old village chief said with a laugh, pointing at his young grandson.

“I remember those years. Niu Kui Mountain was plagued by tigers. For a time, the authorities conscripted and organized us hunters to go into the mountains to eliminate the tigers…”

It hadn’t just been this region of Ning’an County, bordering Niu Kui Mountain and Desheng Prefecture, that had gone through these events. Similar situations occurred in the other two prefectures adjacent to Niu Kui Mountain. However, the situation here in Ning’an County was more severe. The old village chief, however, didn’t have access to news from prefectures so far away.

“Back then, I was still in good shape, and I went into the mountains twice with that group. We even found a tiger’s lair and personally hunted one. However, we didn’t find any human bones in the vicinity. But then…”

The old village chief showed a hint of lingering fear.

“There’s an old saying: ‘the wind follows the tiger, clouds follow the dragon.’ Everyone has heard of it, but no one has ever seen it. But that time, we saw it! ”

“That night, about thirty of our experienced hunters and those of the neighboring villages were waiting together on a remote mountain ridge where we were had determined a fierce tiger had roamed… Originally, the wind was gentle and the moon bright, but suddenly, a fierce gale began to rage, dust and rocks obscured our vision, and the roar of the fierce tiger echoed in the air. That howling sound made our hearts tremble with fear; even the bravest hunters would be scared stiff.”

The young grandson had unconsciously moved closer to his mother, not even chewing the meat in his mouth anymore.

“But we were all hunters; no matter what, we had to remain calm. No one dared to make a sound. But that great tiger seemed to know exactly where we were. With a casual pounce, it snatched away a hunter from the neighboring village. His screams echoed all along as we chased after it, but it disappeared without a trace. What was even more odd was…”

The old village chief deliberately leaned closer to his young grandson.

“The wind had also stopped! ”

“Hey, old man, why are you scaring the child? You old rascal!”

“Hahaha… Doesn’t that child love hearing about this, though?”

“His little face has turned pale from fright! ”

The hunter quickly refilled his father-in-law’s wine.

“Dad, please continue. ”

“Well, when we came back, we were quite disheartened and thoroughly scared. But as long as the man-eating tiger wasn’t eliminated, no one could rest easy. The government’s reward was substantial, and they even dispatched officers proficient in martial arts to join the effort. Furthermore, some of us hunters were unwilling to give up, so we went again. ”

At this point, the old village chief sighed.

“Sigh… my brother also died that time. That fierce tiger was truly monstrous; it playfully snatched away two men and even killed seven hunting dogs. It came and went enveloped in mountain winds, and its roars made anyone who heard them weak in the knees. After that, I and some of the old hunters understood clearly that we were no match at all. From then on, no matter how much the authorities tried to persuade us, we refused to enter the mountains again…”

The old village chief took a sip of wine to calm his nerves.

“The story of the mountain tiger becoming a spirit also began to spread after that incident. But our villages here kept it a closely guarded secret; no one spoke carelessly, afraid of attracting its attention.”

“Later, I heard that the government posted official notices and even went to Desheng Prefecture to seek aid and spread the word. Afterwards, some formidable martial artists arrived. As expected of martial arts experts, they were indeed superior; they actually managed to kill the fierce tiger, and it was a rare white tiger at that! Its hide was displayed at the county government office entrance for many days. Many people from our village went to see it! ”

The hunter frowned, gripped his chopsticks, and drained the rice wine in his cup in one gulp. Then asked a question.

“Dad, are you sure that fierce tiger really died? ”

“Of course! That white tiger was absolutely a ferocious creature, two ordinary tigers couldn’t even compare! And since then, Niu Kui Mountain has been peaceful. Forget man-eating tigers, we’ve hardly even seen any common tigers! ”

The hunter hesitated for a moment, then lowered his head and asked again in a hushed tone.

“Dad, is there any possibility that that tiger spirit isn’t dead yet? ”

The old village chief’s scalp tingled instinctively. He turned his head and stared fixedly at his son-in-law.

“What nonsense are you spouting? Did you… did you see it? ”

“No, no, I didn’t. It’s just that this time when we were going down the mountain, the thunder sounded like the roar of a fierce beast, and the dark clouds in the sky were terrifying. All the wild animals were running out of the depths of the mountains, and my heart, well… I was just really unnerved…”

Both the old village chief and the hunter looked out through the window. The deep mountains were obscured, so they couldn’t see too far. They could only look up at the sky.

At this moment.

“Crack… boom…”

A deafening clap of thunder, a thick bolt of lightning, like a speak with three prongs, struck directly into the depths of the mountain.

Everyone in the entire mountain village shivered at the same moment.

Chapter 343: On Behalf of My Master, I Ask the Heavens

“Bang, bang, bang, bang…”

The thunderous roar caused the large bowl of braised pork knuckle on the table to shake violently. After a while, the quiet room began to fill with sound again, but this time no one dared to talk about monsters anymore.

There was an old saying: don’t gossip about people during the day, don’t gossip about ghosts at night. At this moment, the old village chief’s house was observing a similar superstition.

The Niu Kui Mountain Range stretched for over two hundred li, crossing the border of Desheng Prefecture, passing through Dingyuan Prefecture, and brushing past Tianyue Prefecture. As the expanse covered by the thunderclouds in the depths of the mountains grew, some supernatural entities frequently gazed in the direction of Niu Kui Mountain.

In Ning’an County and Baoshun County of Desheng Prefecture, Chengze County and Caiming County of Dingyuan Prefecture, and Dakang County of Tianyue Prefecture—all the large counties relatively close to Niu Kui Mountain—golden statues of deities appeared in the City God Temples of their county towns. All the City Gods of each county appeared on the highest point of their temples, watching the rolling thunderclouds deep within Niu Kui Mountain.

Of these counties, Ning’an County was the closest to the center of the thunderstorm.

“Boom…”

As dawn broke, another bolt of lightning of extraordinary power cleaved down from the thunderclouds, striking Niu Kui Mountain like a sharp sword.

At the top of the City God Temple in Ning’an County, the old City God Song Shichang stood solemnly, looking at Niu Kui Mountain with a heavy expression, saying nothing.

“Your Honor, this lightning doesn’t seem to be from an ordinary thunderstorm. It might be a supernatural sign from heaven an earth. Either a supreme treasure has appeared in Niu Kui Mountain, or something extraordinary has happened! It’s even possible that a terrifying demon is about to attain enlightenment!”

The head official of the Yin-Yang Bureau advised the Old City God, deeply concerned.

“Yes, these thunderclouds are indeed unusual, and they do resemble some kind of tribulation cloud, but the might of heaven is unpredictable in such a situation!”

At this moment, in the distance, silver lightning danced like snakes in the distance. Some peals of thunder roared loudly, while others were weaker. Some of the thunderclaps were so powerful that even ghosts and gods felt immense pressure.

The Old City God subconsciously glanced in a certain direction in the county town. At this moment, after nightfall, the visibility was very low due to the dark clouds and heavy rain, but a faint, ethereal light still flickered in one corner of town.

In that direction lay Ju’an Pavilion.

“But there’s no need to worry too much. That gentleman resides in Ning’an County, and he frequently visits Niu Kui Mountain. If there were truly some evil entity in the mountain about to achieve a crucial breakthrough in its cultivation, how could it possibly escape that gentleman’s discerning eyes?”

The Martial Judge on the other side stroked his beard.

“Who knows, perhaps it is even related to him!”

“Could it be that red fox? No way, that fox’s cultivation level is far too low, isn’t it?”

“How dare we criticize the actions of such beings? Even if that gentleman is calm and serene, you all must be careful with your words!”

“Yes, let’s not discuss this further.”

…

At this moment, at the foot of Dragon Rock Island in the southeast corner of the Northern Sea, Ji Yuan lay on his side in the guest wing, resting. His eyes were lightly closed, his eyelids constantly twitching. With his mind focused, the mountains and rivers of his inner world appeared in a misty state.

Ji Yuan was in a state between dream and reality, a special state that had occurred several times before. In a daze, he felt a spiritual connection with Niu Kui Mountain in western Ji Province.

His gaze seemed to pierce through mountains and rivers, revealing the scene there: rolling leaden clouds, flashing lightning, and rumbling thunder.

Strangely, in other parts of Niu Kui Mountain, and even in the surrounding villages and counties, the places shrouded in dark clouds experienced both thunder and rain, but here, there was only wind and thunder, no rain.

Lightning bolts swirled and flowed within the clouds, some below, some above, some inside, some emerging from the void, converging from all directions at the center of the thunderclouds, brewing into twisted and unpredictable charged mass of lightning.

“Crack… Boom…”

A white light accompanied by a deafening roar filled the sky as a bolt of lightning struck down from the thunderclouds. It twisted and flashed as it touched a spot on the mountain in an instant.

“Roar—“

As lightning illuminated the deep mountains, a roaring beast faced the sky, seemingly transforming into a beam of ghostly light as it charged towards the thunder.

“Boom…”

A storm of lghtning surged through surrounding mountains and forests, darting like silver snakes across the mountainside cliffs and ridges.

‘What courage! He didn’t even try to dodge the lightning tribulation!’

Ji Yuan, witnessing this scene in a daze, couldn’t help but admire it in his mind.

Cultivating meant defying the heavens, and lightning was a symbol of divine power. Plants, animals, and birds in particular feared lightning. Spirits that had awakened their intelligence would instinctively hide and seek refuge during thunderstorms, as if they had a deep-seated fear rooted in their souls.

When a spirit or monster, especially a demon, was about to gain power, its location would often attract thunderstorms, sometimes even out of season, as if their actions were against the will of Heaven.

However, even with such terrifying lightning, it was still merely weather. True demons possessed considerable intelligence and didn’t necessarily have to be struck by lightning when it was at its strongest. They could easily avoid it to consolidate their cultivation. By the time their cultivation stage was complete, their aura would be much calmer, and the lightning tribulation would be much weaker.

At that time, they could find a more suitable place to withstand the lightning, and then it would be much safer.

But Lu Shanjun was clearly not like that. Ji Yuan knew his tiger very well; rather than saying he didn’t know when to advance and when to retreat, it was more accurate to say he was extremely confident and proud.

On Niu Kui Mountain, on the platform where Ji Yuan lectured to Lu Shanjun, a hairless giant tiger lay prostrate on a disc-shaped boulder, gazing at the sky. The tiger had many scorch marks on its body, but they seemed to have no effect on it.

However, only three true tribulation lightning bolts had struck, each stronger than the last, and the thunderclouds in the sky continued to expand.

“Rumble…”

“Rumble…”

“Rumble…”

Ordinary lightning bolts continued to fall, occasionally striking the tiger, but the tiger neither dodged nor avoided them. Although he was in some pain, he knew that, compared to the real test, ordinary lightning was merely a tickle.

“Roar–“

The tiger’s roar echoed throughout the mountains, making the already fierce winds rage even harder. The clouds in the sky seemed even heavier. Looking up from the promontory, it felt as though the clouds were swirling and converging towards the center.

At this moment, the charred skin on his body felt itchy and painful, but Lu Shanjun couldn’t care less.

“Crackle… Crackle…”

In the sky, dark clouds gathered and thunder rumbled, the crackling of electricity clearly audible even on the ground.

Lu Shanjun’s pupils contracted as he noticed that the central lightning was faintly tinged with blood, sending a chill down his spine.

‘Run away, run away, run away…’

This thought rose uncontrollably, but the tiger’s facial expression grew increasingly ferocious and terrifying.

“Heh…heh heh…”

The tiger snarled, revealing pale white fangs and sharp teeth. The beast bared its teeth threateningly at the sky, its sharp claws snapping out, its skin rippling like waves, its muscles bulging out menacingly.

The next moment, the tiger’s four paws slowly lifted off the ground, and a gust of wind swirled around him as he rose several dozen feet away from the platform. Lu Shanjun, who should not have been able to fly yet, rose up as if stepping on thin air, and roared fiercely at the blood-red lightning that was about to fall from the sky.

“Roar–“

“Crack… Boom…”

The thunderbolt struck almost simultaneously with the tiger’s roar, piercing straight through the ominous wind that enveloped the tiger and struck its body.

“Boom…”

The tiger was directly slammed onto the platform below by the crimson lightning, which not only coiled around it but also enveloped the entire platform.

Immediately afterwards, another bolt of blood-red lightning struck down, directly hitting the tiger on the promontory.

“Crack… Rumble…”

“Awooooo…”

Lu Shanjun let out a bloodcurdling scream.

At this moment, all the beasts in Niu Kui Mountain were silent. The birds had retreated to their nests, and countless animals tried their best to hide in places that could shelter them from the wind and rain, trembling in the face of the thunderous might.

Hu Yun also huddled in his fox den, which was located halfway up the mountainside on a high peak. Besides the warm nest in the center, there were two entrances, one large and one small, one facing Ning’an County to one side and the other facing the depths of Niu Kui Mountain on the other.

Hu Yun was looking through the cave entrance toward the depths of Niu Kui Mountain, anxiously watching the lightning strikes. However, from his perspective, he couldn’t see the crimson lightning; instead, it looked like ordinary lightning.

Shanjun, Shanjun, please don’t get hurt! The Master isn’t here. If anything happens to you, no one can save you!”

“Awooooo…”

A tiger’s faint scream rang out. Hu Yun trembled and his red fur bristled.

…

On the platform, Lu Shanjun lay halfway on the boulder. Half his face was charred, his body covered in patches of unscathed skin. In some places, the charred skin peeled off bit by bit with the electric arcs on his body. His body would tremble from time to time, and streaks of tiger blood seeped out from his wounds. It was a truly devastating sight.

“Heh…heh…heh heh heh…”

Half of Lu Shanjun’s lip was charred and fell off, so his fangs were exposed. He propped himself up on his forelimbs and looked up above his head again. The patches of charred skin mixed with the blood dripping from his head fell one after another, revealing deeper layers of stripes beneath the flesh. His tiger eyebrows were more prominent and longer, and his nose was narrower and longer…

The sixth bolt of True Lightning was brewing in the sky. This lightning twisted and changed in Lu Shanjun’s vision, its color also shifting uncertainly, appearing both blue and red…

“Even if I misjudged the power of your tribulation lightning, even if I, Lu Shanjun, am to perish here today… I will not… I will not disgrace my master…”

The tiger trembled as it struggled to its feet, shaking off the blood.

Even though he had already grasped the mysteries of enlightenment and was only one step away from it, Lu Shanjun was somewhat defiant. However, when he looked at the lightning in the sky, he felt an overwhelming sense of inescapable danger, as if even fleeing back into the cave would be futile.

“A man that understands the Tao in the morning, can die content in the evening…1 Hah…”

The tiger straightened its cold body, its gaze coldly fixed on the thunderclouds in the sky. The remaining monstrous energy in his body surged, and his aura reached its peak since the tribulation started. It was not a tiger’s roar, nor a fierce cry, but rather a shout that seemed to be howling towards the sky.

“On behalf of my revered master, I ask the Heavens: who commands the thunder?”

“…Who commands the thunder?”

“…Who commands…?”

Lu Shanjun’s roar echoed throughout Niu Kui Mountain, and reverberated between heaven and earth.

朝聞道，夕死可矣 Quote by Confucius

Chapter 344: Are You Lu Shanjun?

Ji Yuan, far away in the Underwater Palace on Dragon Rock Island in the Northern Sea, frowned deeply. The lightning tribulation that Lu Shanjun was facing was too exaggerated, many times worse than the lightning tribulations of the monsters he knew.

In particular, it was unheard of for the lightning to change color from red to blue. How could this be the kind of heavenly lightning that ordinary demons could avoid?

If every transformed monster had to face such heavenly lightning, it would be almost impossible for any of them to survive.

Why did Lu Shanjun need to face such divine lightning? Was it because he was about to be reborn and transform into another kind of being?

Even dragons and flood dragons…

No! The Dragon Transformation Tribulation was equally extraordinary. The dragon races of this world had even gathered great wisdom and developed the supernatural technique of “Water Walking” in their long history to gradually resist the Dragon Transformation Tribulation.

But that wasn’t a transformation into a flood dragon, but rather into a True Dragon. No matter how powerful Lu Shanjun’s previous body was, he was just a fierce tiger spirit that had refined his horizontal bones, far from reaching the level of an old flood dragon attempting a True Dragon transformation.

Ji Yuan’s mind raced as he rapidly considered the cause and effect, but now was clearly not the time to overthink it. He couldn’t grasp the key reasoning in an instant, nor could he continue to stand aside any longer.

‘Lu Shanjun absolutely cannot die!’

This wasn’t just a matter of Ji Yuan’s chess strategy, but also because he was Ji Yuan’s only disciple; how could there not be emotional factors involved?

Within the vast chessboard of his inner world, Ji Yuan’s towering figure materialized, reaching towards the sky to retrieve a dazzling black chess piece, from which the faint sound of wind and thunder could be heard.

With a wave of his hand, Ji Yuan drew in wisps of mysterious yellow energy around the alchemy furnace atop his inner world’s mountain peak, which gathered in thin yellow ribbons.

Then, at this moment, as he was connected to Lu Shanjun, Ji Yuan’s massive dharma form closed his eyes and concentrated for a moment, holding the black chess piece that shone like a star.

The next moment, he abruptly opened his eyes, and at the same time, heaven and earth were reborn…

The world around him shifted and changed like smoke and mist, and the surrounding scenery of mountains and rivers taking on different shapes, somewhat resembling Niu Kui Mountain.

Ji Yuan held the chess piece in his sword-fingers. The mysterious yellow energy at his fingertips seemed to rotate with him, and he quickly pointed downwards. The flames of his alchemy furnace ignited, and the spiritual energy surged, nurturing heaven and earth. His thoughts were elusive, seemingly casual but in truth exerting all his strength. Although the fog and blurriness before his eyes was thick, the promontory seemed to materialize before him like something from a dream.

“Lu Shanjun, let your master lend you a hand!”

…

On Niu Kui Mountain, atop the giant rock platform, a fierce tiger roared with unwavering resolve, its daring question echoing in the sky. Above, the clouds were brewing a new thunderbolt, this one a deadly strike.

At that very moment, a vast and boundless voice echoed in the ears of the fierce tiger spirit Lu Shanjun.

“Lu Shanjun, let your master lend you a hand!”

The tiger looked around in surprise, but the voice seemed to have come out of nowhere.

“Master!?”

Immediately afterwards, wisps of faint, mysterious yellow energy, began to press down and swirl like a vortex, with a heavy yet non-threatening pressure.

The sky still crackled with lightning, but at this moment, Lu Shanjun closed his eyes. The surrounding colors did not fade; instead, he “saw” a new scene.

A gigantic, illusory hand, enveloped in endless mist, pointed towards him with sword fingers extended, its arm radiating a mysterious aura, clad in a familiar long-sleeved blue robe.

Thump…

A dull thud struck his forehead, as if a giant finger had tapped it.

Swish…

Streams of primordial energy flowed into his forehead at the touch, permeating his limbs and bones, before ultimately settling into his bone marrow.

“Roar—”

Lu Shanjun roared again. Although he did not feel any recovery in his physical strength or spiritual energy, a kind of power was rising within him, as if he had regained the confidence to fight against the lightning tribulation.

With another tiger roar, the image in his mind quickly faded, and a sudden realization dawned on him.

‘Master is not nearby; he is assisting from afar using some kind of supernatural power.’

“Crackle…crack, crackle…”

The bones in his body emitted strange noises. The parts that had already undergone transformation after being struck by several heavenly lightning bolts began to grow rapidly. His entire tiger body was trembling slightly, and subtle deformations appeared in his spine and head.

“Roar… Awoo… Roar…”

The roars grew deeper and deeper, but each roar contained a suppressed power, and a powerful aura swept across the entire platform.

‘Something’s missing, something’s still missing…’

Lu Shanjun trembled uncontrollably, knowing in his heart that the moment of his true transformation was about to arrive, but something was still missing.

“Boom…”

Thunder rumbled overhead, causing Lu Shanjun to look up abruptly. From within the dark clouds, a bolt of lightning, glowing with a bluish-red light, had already materialized.

‘You’re the only thing missing!’

“Crack… Boom…”

A bluish-white light filled the sky, illuminating everything in Niu Kui Mountain and the surrounding counties and towns. Whether ghost, or god, or mortal, everyone heard the deafening final thunderclap.

The lightning struck that very moment, hitting Lu Shanjun directly on the forehead, like a torrent of heavenly electricity, forcing the giant tiger down onto his knees on the promontory.

“Rumble rumble rumble rumble…”

Lightning crackled around them, igniting large swathes of forest, but before the fire could fully take hold, everything was charred in an instant.

Even more intense pain washed over Lu Shanjun; the threat of death had never vanished, but it could no longer maintain an absolute advantage over him.

“Roar… Roar…!”

The tiger trembled as he rose amidst the lightning, his skin rapidly charring and turning black, his blood boiling and becoming even more viscous.

“Sizzle sizzle…”

“Crackle crackle crackle…”

The lightning and the sound of bones rubbing together continued for a long time before finally stopping—or rather, everything fell silent, leaving only the distant sound of rain.

Lu Shanjun stood motionless on the platform, completely transformed into a charred tiger statue.

Whoooo… Whoooosh…

A mountain breeze blew.

Suddenly, all the black char on his body crumbled away. Lu Shanjun seemed to have lost a lot of weight. His bones were exposed, and although some of his body was still wrapped inside the bones, he still stood on the giant stone platform like a terrifying skeleton, seemingly emaciated.

“Ba-dum… Ba-dum… ba-dum…”

A sound like a sledgehammer striking a drum emanated from inside the giant tiger’s skeleton; it was the sound of a powerful heartbeat.

With energy of the New Year, all things are born anew.

From within the giant tiger’s heart, a surge of springtime energy, originally gathered with the help of the immortal sword, emanated and flowed throughout his entire body.

The giant tiger’s life force began to spread again with newly created blood.

Little by little, piece by piece, the tissue all over Lu Shanjun’s body began to regrow.

His form remained tiger-like, yet somewhat different.

New fur began to grow, a deep black and yellow, but the downy hair on the face became extremely short, and the area between the nose and muzzle became more slender. The eyes were no longer round and tiger-like, but narrow and long with a black striped eyebrow, resembling a pair of human eyes. His triangular ears became more rounded, and two long black and white intertwined hairs grew under each ear, with the ends puffing up into small tufts, like a pair of special long earrings.

His entire face resembled both a tiger and a human.

“Crackle…crackle…”

As his bones stretched and his flesh grew, Lu Shanjun’s body continued to grow, already far exceeding the size of his previous enormous tiger body.

A deep, mottled black and yellow tail swung behind him again, seemingly trailing wisps of smoke, like the phantom of many tails.

“Heh…heh heh heh…”

The pain in his body was gradually subsiding, replaced by a tingling, itchy, yet very comfortable feeling.

Beneath Dragon Rock Island in the Northern Sea, Ji Yuan, somewhat alarmed, held the chess piece and sensed Lu Shanjun’s changes. He had a strange suspicion about this appearance, but he wasn’t quite certain.

As the tribulation ended, and with Ji Yuan’s mind still slightly shaken, the connection between him and the chess pieces at this crucial moment was rapidly weakening and was about to sever, which was something Ji Yuan couldn’t control.

Fortunately, everything that needed to be done had been done, and Lu Shanjun had successfully overcome his tribulation. The next moment, the connection was finally broken, and Ji Yuan, somewhat exhausted, was finally able to get some sleep and rest.

However, on Niu Kui Mountain, Lu Shanjun’s transformation was far from over.

About half an hour later, a giant beast that resembled a tiger but was a tiger no longer appeared on the platform. Lu Shanjun didn’t need any mirror to understand his new appearance.

‘Is this what Master meant by a complete transformation?’

As this thought crossed his mind, Lu Shanjun stretched comfortably on the giant rock of the platform. His already enormous body appeared even more imposing and massive, and his long fur undulated like waves with each movement, faintly radiating a halo of light.

Then, Lu Shanjun rose up on his hind legs, his two front paws clasped together in front of his face.

Whoosh…whoosh…whoosh…

The mountain wind picked up again, blowing away the black ash surrounding the giant beast. Suddenly, Lu Shanjun separated his two giant paws.

In this instant, spiritual energy flowed through his body, and his entire body glowed with an unyielding light. A stream of light rose from his face and enveloped his entire body.

It seemed as if endless gold dust and light were scattered all over him, with most of his body was like powder flowing with the wind. The radiance, though increasing in intensity, did not spread, but instead contracted inward.

The entire behemoth seemed to transform into a faint light, shrinking smaller and smaller until it finally took on a human form.

A quarter of an hour after the thunder stopped, Hu Yun, who had been hiding in his cave, could no longer contain himself. Plus he had not heard Lu Shanjun’s roar again, and in his anxiety the red fox had already run out of the cave and ran into the depths of Niu Kui Mountain, towards the platform where he had once listened to a lecture.

At that very moment, he happened to arrive at the platform and witnessed the transformation in the flowing light.

When the radiance faded, Hu Yun became the first witness to Lu Shanjun’s human form.

The red fox stared, wide-eyed and gaping, gazing blankly at the “person” before him. His red fur stood on end, and one of his paws was raised, pointing at the promontory.

“You, are you Lu Shanjun!?”

The person on the platform turned around, smiled at the red fox that stood not too far away, and cupped his hands in a gesture of respect towards him.

“I am indeed Lu!”

At this moment, Lu Shanjun had a jade-like face and black hair, a slender figure, and wore a pale yellow wide-sleeved robe adorned with black cloud patterns. He looked calm and handsome, as refined as a young scholar, and elegant and graceful as a gentleman.

“No! You must have transformed incorrectly; you shouldn’t look like this!”

Hu Yun now understood transformation quite well. It wasn’t an illusion, but rather a true human form. It was not something you could just choose to take on; it had to be something in harmony with one’s own heart and the Tao.

Hu Yun found it absurd that Lu Shanjun’s human form would be so “weak.” Shouldn’t he be a muscular giant with arms thicker than other people’s thighs, a waist as large as a millstone, and a head that could smash through a wall?

The man on the platform came out of his bow, waved his sleeve, and tucked his hands at his sides, ignoring Hu Yun’s astonishment. Instead, he looked up again at the dark clouds in the sky, which no longer showed any sign of lightning. The aura of the tribulation was dissipating.

“I ask Heaven once more, who commands the thunder?”

His voice was calm and even, yet extremely loud, echoing through the mountains for a long time, but again, there was no reply.

“Drip drip drip…”

The lightning tribulation had passed, and the dark clouds it left behind eventually turned into an ordinary thunderstorm. Originally, there had been no rain here, but now large raindrops began to fall, nourishing the mountains and earth that had been ravaged by lightning throughout the night.
